- Merges stop failing on chance: the self-change gate judges each hook's median CPU time instead of its slowest run, and holds a rise only when every timing pair shows it, while a hook that waits (its wall median rising) or slows only in its tail (p95 up 50% and 25 ms) still holds; benched against itself, an unchanged tree swung up to +78% on the old basis, and ten merges in a day were held on hooks their diffs never touched (T-3131).
- Smith on local models gets `--reason off|state|native`: `state` carries a compact thought state between turns and turns thinking on only on low confidence, a failed check or a loop break, and asks for the named check before an edit; `models.complete` applies a thinking budget with a forced close where the backend allows it; `sampo exam run --reason` scores each arm. First runs: native thinking ran out of budget in 6 of 6 gemma 12B runs, state in 1 of 3 (T-3006).
- Smith gets one more try at a refused plan: when `sampo focus` refuses a local model's plan (a request clause with no criterion, a missing Undo line), the planner sees the refusal and answers once more; a corrected plan is filed and the refused task dropped, and a second refusal ends the run with both reasons as before (T-3016).
- `sampo-decide`, the first model trained on Sampo's own outcomes: a LoRA on Jeff v1.3 from `sampo exam models --train-jeff`, labelled only by user tags, realized size and whether a close held, split by whole projects (largest first toward 70/15/15, so every fold gets rows), trained with a small-data recipe, and kept at the step dev loss picked. On its held-out project it called tier right 0.64 of the time against the majority's 0.12 (T-2970).
- Moons count what's kept: `sampo moons grow --keep N` runs grow and funnel rounds until N moons survive (stopping after three rounds that raise no survivor), the night grows while kept moons are under 10,000, and families union links the novelty scan already computes, so 10,000 survivors group in about a second instead of ten minutes (T-3046).
- Exam runs reach keyed models: the secrets file's default path comes from the account's home, not `$HOME`, which an exam sandbox points at a throwaway, so the 27B arm no longer scores 0 on "LLM_API_KEY is not set" (T-3080).
- `sampo drive on --pace off` now turns off every weekly-usage lean for its project: past 80% weekly the budget caps no longer halve, subagents and builder lanes keep running, and the pane's economy mode stays off; the 5-hour window keeps its 90% rule, and other projects lean as before (T-3049).
- A model profile's key is found without a login shell: when its `key_env` isn't in the environment, Sampo reads it from `~/.config/sampo/secrets.env` (or `$SAMPO_SECRETS`), a private file it trusts only at mode 600, so hooks, the night job and the live service reach keyed models such as the 27B on Jarvis (T-3036).
- The live page manages every Sampo session on the machine from one address: http://127.0.0.1:46459/ opens with no link for your own user (the server checks the connection's uid through /proc/net/tcp, matching the full address and port pair; anyone else still needs the link). A Sessions rail and a Projects strip list every live session and project, and picking one switches the scene to it (T-3022, T-3023, T-3024, T-3027).
- The live page steers sessions. Each project plate has drive on/off, checkpoint and resume, and each session row has a note. In the rail, pause now (a second press confirms it) and pause off sit beside a status line that shows each result. Every action runs a fixed `sampo` verb and is logged in that project's ledger, behind JSON-only, own-Origin, Sec-Fetch-Site and page-token checks. A port forward you run (ssh -L and the like) makes its remote clients look like your uid, so they are stopped only by the token (T-3026).
- On the live page, a local model that is called but not loaded right now hangs on the gantry, not on Jarvis's hill: each call row records whether it went to a local or a remote endpoint (T-3017).
- `sampo exam run` refuses a second run of the same run and agent while one is running, naming its pid and model, so two `--model` arms can't mix their answers (T-3029).
- Sampo Code's terminal UI and the pane speak the forge's heat language: what runs glows in fire colours, what is finished cools to steel, and checks that passed stay green. The TUI's night and paper themes take the forge's colours, its banner reads `⊖ SAMPO CODE`, and the pane's mark is the anvil-stone (`⊖` at rest, `⊖ ⊘` while a turn runs). cb, riso, signal and a saved custom palette look exactly as before, and the new TUI preset `classic` brings back the old night (T-2876).
- Sampo reads the context an Ollama model is really served (`/api/ps`, else `/api/show`) and uses it instead of what `models.json` claims; a Smith prompt that wouldn't fit stops before the call with the `ollama create NAME-32k` fix, instead of a run on a silently truncated prompt (T-2935).
- `sampo exam taxonomy` names how each smith scenario ended (reasoning used the whole budget, plan refused, no valid plan, finish refused, a loop, a timeout, closed or other), one end per scenario, summing to the scenarios; a plan that never validated no longer counts as malformed (T-2926).
- A user's M or L plan names each clause of their request that no criterion checks before work starts, and the unproven rollup splits meaning by whose words the clause is, the user's or Sampo's own notes (T-2914).
- MASTER.md opens whole in one Read: each long section lives in a `docs/map` shard, MASTER.md keeps a one-line-per-entry index with links, and every check that reads MASTER.md reads the shards too (T-2915).
- `sampo ideas --model local` runs its lens children on an own or loopback model, with Haiku as the fallback (T-2910). A local-model arm predicts the user's next request (type, paths and words) from recent asks, the queue and the standing steers, scored beside the transition guess and the naive baseline and shown only when it leads (T-2912).
- `sampo night` adds zero-dollar `moons grow` and `moons funnel` jobs while a local model answers: grow while the catalogue is under its 10,000 target, funnel while any candidate waits on a stage; `--local-only` stops a call no local model can answer instead of sending it to any paid model (T-2916). The funnel's kill stage judges as many batches at once as the local profile has slots (T-2953).
- `sampo moons regen PATH` deletes one of Sampo's own modules in a throwaway worktree and regrows it on the local model from its intent alone (its briefs, map entry, tests and callers' names), landing nothing and reporting each proof; the regrown code's tests run without credentials and with a throwaway HOME, a symlinked path can't reach outside the worktree, and a model that is down moves the slice on to the next profile (T-2918).
- `sampo playbooks eval` runs on a local profile; a local writer drafts a small-model variant of a playbook and its eval briefs, and the variant is kept only when it lifts that model at least as much as the original (T-2917).
- Smith asks a local model for each close note finish would refuse (a RESEARCH Decision, a PERFORMANCE task's numbers, a FIX's regression test) in its own slot with a worked example, so a verified task isn't refused at close; an answer that copies the example is asked again, and a run with no model (`--worker` with `--verify`) is blocked with finish's own reason (T-2937). The plan ladder reads the type or tier anywhere in a sentence answer ("The task is a FIX"), not only as its first word (T-2936).
- Core protection holds on the home's real path after `sampo migrate`: with `~/.claude/foreman` a symlink to `~/.claude/sampo`, a write through either path is judged core, and the standing yes covers it the same way; before, a write through the new path wasn't core at all. `sampo migrate --undo` restores a home used since the move and warns about the changed state instead of sticking, and `fm`'s deprecation line reads "fm is now sampo" (T-2967).
- A cold review of the shipped site (a different model, 4 widths, both themes) found 1 High, 4 Medium and 5 Low; fixed: the tower's rule cards (the site's generic `.rules > div` grid had collapsed them to titles at 820 px and up), the landing hero's 90 px drop and wrapping eyebrow, the hammer smear's clipped edge, the Smith section's dead margin at 768, two AA contrast misses on the Code page, the tower's moons table and header width, the Code page's clipped command box, and the pieces diagram at 390 px. The feature pages carrying the forge too is T-2965 (T-2964).
- The site carries the converged illustrated forge (critique 7: nothing above Low left): the landing's How section is one side-on night road with each of the eight stations lit by its own lamp, its props at the hero's scale and the station shown matching the row you read; the Rune tower gains its round 3 and 4 refinements; the hero's italic line is gold gradient text again (T-2957).
- Smith no longer shrinks a local profile or refuses its prompt when Ollama states no context for an unloaded model (a server-wide `OLLAMA_CONTEXT_LENGTH` is invisible to it): the served context reads unknown instead of 4096, `fm code` probes it once a run, and a `ctx_variant` profile still gets its `num_ctx` variant when the context is unknown (T-2947).
- Small-model cards are parsed leniently and checked strictly: a card whose traps come as one string of sentences, or whose steps come as plain strings with a `backticked` command, is read as lists before the checks run. Before, every card the local 12B wrote was refused for its shape (400 calls, 0 kept); now about 1 in 6 passes the strict checks, and the first long grow is running (T-2984).
- Louhi's model bench gains a retrieval decider: for a held-out brief, the 7 nearest closed briefs vote on its type and tier, embedded with bge-m3 on local Ollama or matched by BM25, and the decider abstains when fewer than 4 agree. On 200 held-out briefs it scores 0.77 on type where it answers (92% of them), against 0.60 for majority and 0.73 for the best earlier decider. On tier it scores 0.71, the first tier result to beat majority. The vector cache lives in Sampo's state, not the repo (T-2975).
- The small-model card grower sends a refused card back once with the reason it was refused (a chained command, an unknown first word, a vague step, too few traps or steps). It keeps the repaired card when it passes the same strict checks; the grow line counts repairs (T-2985).
- `sampo moons funnel` and `sampo moons families` lock the moons store. A second run exits at once and names the holder's pid, instead of judging the same candidates twice. `sampo moons funnel --status` prints each stage's kept, rejected and waiting counts without calling a model (T-2986).
- The training lake: `sampo exam models --export DIR` writes one table of finished tasks across every Sampo project on the machine, for Sampo's own models. Each row is labelled by its outcome (held, reverted, fixed later, settling), carries a time split and a weight per project, and has project names, forge owners, the OS user and absolute paths stripped. Agent-written fields are off by default, and sensitive and opted-out projects are never read. The manifest's content hash makes an export reproducible. The first export holds 2,694 tasks from 13 projects (T-2974).
- `sampo task ac add` warns when a verify names `state/projects/…` relative to the repo. State is git-ignored, so a builder lane never holds it, and `sampo lane merge` would read the criterion red; the warning asks for the absolute path (T-2988).
- Exam grading asks the local model about only the newest 30 held-out requests, so a wedged local server costs at most 30 calls per grade instead of one per held-out request. The session-start "you may ask next" row now records which arm made the guess (the transition rule or the local model), and its hit rate is scored by that arm (T-2950).
- A plan is held to the "every clause of the request has a criterion" rule only when the request is the user's own words. A brief whose request carries a provenance line Sampo wrote, or that a Genesis mission filed, is no longer refused for clauses it never meant. A long request's unchecked clauses fold into one line that names three and counts the rest (T-2951).
- A close-holds verifier: `sampo exam models --verifier` trains a small logistic head on the training lake's outcome labels (held, or later fixed or reverted). It uses text-free features only: evidence commands, red-then-green proofs, test totals parsed from commands Sampo ran, lenses, scope, time, tier and type. Whole projects are held out. `sampo task finish` logs its score in shadow, and once the model bench adopts it, a low score prints the missing evidence as advice; it never blocks a close (T-2993). First live run: 590 closes, held-out AUC 0.55 pooled (0.73 on the largest project), so it stays in shadow (T-2977).
- Leave-one-project-out model measurement: `sampo exam models --loo` holds each project out whole, reports coverage and a Wilson bound per contestant, and names its baseline (today's path or the train majority). `--adopt` makes a use live only when the bootstrap lower bound over 3 or more held-out projects beats that baseline and no project regresses. A live use whose agreement drops goes back to shadow, and a flip report names each change between runs (T-2976).
- Exam scenarios minted from real projects: `sampo exam gen --from-repo PATH` takes the repo's HEAD without touching its checkout. It plants one deterministic one-line bug per scenario (a flipped comparison, a dropped guard, an off-by-one bound) and keeps it only when the project's own tests catch it and pass twice without it. Untracked deps (.venv, node_modules) are linked rather than installed, `uv run` is unwrapped so the real venv is never re-synced, and a Rust run shares one cargo target. `--only GLOB` and `--test-cmd` aim it at one module of a big repo. Minted scenarios are graded like seeded ones. First live runs: 4 Python scenarios from Sampo's lake module and 4 TypeScript from jarvis-code; jarvis-code's tests missed 8 of 12 one-line mutants (T-2978, T-2987).
- A repeat-failure firewall. `sampo check --affected` always runs the standing-rule tests (self-docs, the model rules, left-out names, the rename scan), so a builder meets a missing map entry or a broken rule in its own lane. Every builder brief carries a Standing rules section. The self-change gate benches base and branch interleaved over three pairs and holds only a rise seen in two, so load swings no longer read as regressions. `sampo friction --classes` counts the day's refusals by class and says which repeated; the first day's baseline is 9 self-change holds and 7 merges refused on a red criterion (T-2992).
- The exam keeps the full conversation of each proven Smith run: a passing verify, or a grader's hidden check that the defect is gone. The journal is read only from Smith's own home, never from the repo the model wrote. It is stripped of user names, home owners, temp paths and secrets, while code paths stay, and size-capped without ending mid-call. A run with a Claude slot is never kept. `sampo exam models --traces DIR` exports these as chat JSONL with provenance, the training set for the distilled actor. It refuses a journal with no model on record, and a sensitive or opted-out project; sensitive repos are never minted (T-2995).
- Louhi's exam check runs a minted run's clean suites one scenario at a time and retries a lone failed clean run once before calling the scenario flaky. A real project's suite that passes alone no longer reads as flaky because the check ran copies in parallel. Seeded runs keep the parallel check (T-2991).
- A live page of everything Sampo is doing. `sampo watch --web` serves a read-only page on 127.0.0.1 (token link, cookie, strict CSP) that streams the session: a night-forge scene where the task at the anvil, builders, jobs (furnaces), Jeff, Jarvis and every loaded or called model (lanterns) are drawn, with sparks along each model call. Below it are the GPU tank, furnaces, queue, today's closes, counters for cards and moons, and a ledger ticker in plain words; its fonts ship with it. Every `models.complete` and `models.embed` call is noted in `state/calls.jsonl` (capped), so local writers, Smith runs and Jarvis show up as they happen. It stays still under reduced motion (T-2998, T-2999, T-3014).
- Sampo's first model trained on its own outcomes. `sampo exam models --jeff DIR` writes Jeff decision Examples from the training lake: type only where the user tagged the request, tier from the realized scope size, close-holds from the outcome. Folds are whole projects, and the held-out project is in none. `--train-jeff` trains a rank-16 LoRA on the Jeff v1.3 base on the local GPU. It uses a small-data recipe and the base's own identity, keeps the checkpoint jeff-train selected on dev loss, and refuses while another model holds the GPU. On the one project it never saw, the `sampo-decide` adapter scored 0.64 on tier against 0.12 for majority, and 0.76 on type against 0.72 (T-2970).
- `fm code`'s first line names the project and its root ("fm code in <slug> (<root>)"), and the start event carries both, so a run started with `-p` shows which project it resolved to (T-2741).
- Smith breaks a small model's loop: the third identical action on the same target in a step goes once to the `--planner` (else `--escalate`) profile for a corrected action, then back to the actor; with neither, the actor gets a note naming the repetition, and a `loop_break` event is journaled. Loops were the main end of the second uplift exam (4 of 10 scenarios on both arms) (T-2966).
- `fm code --planner NAME` splits planning from acting: a stronger profile writes the plan and the close notes, and `--model` runs every step and fix round, so a 4B–9B model that loops on a plan of its own acts on one it can follow; `sampo exam run … --agent smith --planner NAME` scores the split beside solo (T-2945). The funnel's tier stage judges batches at once too (T-2959).
- The site build lists only rated moons in the landing's wild list and still counts an unrated one (a new moon waits for `sampo moons rate`); before, one unrated moon in the live tower's data failed the whole build (T-2958).
- `sampo ship` ships a branch that touched a file the live tower keeps publishing to main (`site/genesis/*`, the site's stats file): those files don't count against replaying the branch's own steps, and a step whose only clash is in them keeps main's copy; any other clash still stops the ship. Before, one branch commit to `genesis.json` sent the ship back to cherry-picking 382 commits one by one, which conflicted (T-2955). The same rule holds when the tower publishes while the ship's tests run and the ship re-picks onto the new main (T-2963).
- The guard's refusals say "Ward: blocked …" and its no-task block "Sampo: …"; the output style is named Sampo; the looks page, the hooks listing, the Remote Control session name, the agents-file markers and the plugin READMEs say Sampo, and every reader of the old text (replay, cost, the mod, uninstall) still reads it. The rename scan now covers the guard, the looks page, hooks.json and the READMEs, and its count of leftover old names fell from 19 to 16, every one a reader or a kept protocol string (T-2946).
- `install.sh --local-models` (asked on a terminal, off with none) pulls the pinned Ollama embedders `bge-m3:567m` and `nomic-embed-text:v1.5` when Ollama is installed, else prints its download URL and goes on; `sampo doctor`'s model-backends line names the local embedders and any missing pin with its pull command (T-2880).
- Smith trims a local model's prompt to the context it is served (the file list first, then older results; never the instructions or the request) and says what it left out, instead of sending a prompt the server would cut from the start (T-2939).
- An Ollama profile with `ctx_variant: true` that serves less context than it promises gets a num_ctx variant (`NAME-32k`), made with `ollama create` or reused when already listed, and Smith runs on it (T-2938).
- Louhi's smith arm asks the local model once for its findings (file, symbol) after the run, so a model that names the planted defect is credited as found; a finding naming a file outside the repo is dropped (T-2931).
- `sampo goal plan` reads the document a goal cites (a path and a section): each numbered entry and each DONE-WHEN clause becomes an item, a clause carries its own exam or tower check, repeats are dropped, and `goal verdict` names each unmet item instead of "the goal has no items" (T-2913).
- `sampo playbooks small grow|coverage|show`: cards written for 4B to 27B models, each with when to use it, numbered steps with exact commands, one worked example, a self-check and the traps a small model falls into, grown on the local models thin grid cells first; a card missing a part, or naming a command no tool has, is rejected when it's written (T-2909).
- Louhi's exam runs Sampo's own loop on a local model, `sampo exam run RUN --agent smith --model PROFILE`, graded like the agent arms, and `sampo exam taxonomy RUN` counts how the model failed: a malformed reply, a wrong tool, a loop, done said but not verified, a timeout, a refusal (T-2908).
- The tower and every section page are in the illustrated forge: the carved tower under the moon sky, the Moons funnel and families as text, engraved kickers and forged chart plates; pages name models by role and the orchestrator as any agent (T-2852). The landing is the illustrated forge too, with self-hosted fonts, motion that rests when you stop interacting, stills under reduced motion, and a Moons section built from the catalogue (T-2851).
- A resumed session closes the background work its closed terminal left behind, and the drive pushes again only when its agents change, not on each shell a subagent starts (T-2900, T-2901). `fm code`'s tests no longer take a different path when a local model server runs on the machine (T-2891).
- Local models do the bulk work for real. A profile in `models.json` can set `body` (extra request fields), `think` (reasoning on or off), `max_out` and `slots`. A reply that reasoning cut off with no text now fails with "reasoning used the whole budget" instead of quietly falling back to Haiku, which is what had been happening to every local moon. `sampo moons grow --workers N --think on|off` uses a server's parallel slots and counts calls per model, and `moons coverage` shows kept moons by model, so reasoning on and off can be compared. With `MOONS_LOCAL` unset, the first own or local text-generating profile is used. The funnel embeds through its own profile, `MOONS_EMBED`, which defaults to the best-ranked local embedder (T-2893).
- `sampo exam models run|show` benches local decision models (Ollama's `/v1/systemone`, Jeff, SelfJev) and embedders on held-out closed tasks: accuracy, Brier score and latency per backend, in `research/models/bench.jsonl`. `decide()` speaks Jev's typed-decision API, and the local chat probe never picks a decision or embedding model (T-2887).
- The drive keeps waiting on a subagent that runs past 20 minutes, when it reads its own events and when the engine's list leaves the agent out, and it reads far enough back for long ones; before, a long builder or design round read as a finished service and the session stopped with it still running. A stopped agent no longer counts as running (T-2872, T-2873, T-2874).
- The per-command budget cap now bounds a command's total spend, so one made of many small model calls can't run past it (T-2871).
- `sampo moons grow` and `sampo moons coverage`: candidate moons generated by grid, thin cells first, each with "Today you can't …" and "With this, you can …" lines and a one-day kill test, on a local model when one is keyed, else Haiku (T-2868).
- With no active task, `sampo lease drop` never frees another open task's leases; builder lanes share the session id, and one builder had dropped 16 (T-2870).
- A lane merge now lists changed text still found elsewhere in the code, readers first, so a writer and a reader that drifted apart reach the review (T-2861).
- Hook notes, guard refusals, the drive's reasons, the Next: line and CLI hints print `sampo` spellings; every parser still accepts `fm` (T-2847).
- Under the standing core yes or trust, a core block that's only about the form of a write (a heredoc, a tree write) names the plain-edit route and no longer tells Claude to ask you (T-2859).
- A criterion's verify command may use Sampo's verb names, such as `sampo moons check`; before, the checker refused them as unknown though they ran (T-2866).
- Anticipation gaps wait deferred for the next generation's seal, which closes them, instead of being offered as work (T-2863).
- The experimental macOS CI job gives each test class 900 s before its watchdog; the main jobs keep 480 s (T-2844).
- `sampo outcomes --questions` also counts permission prompts (an `fm ask` answered yes or no) as asks, so `--none` fails when the user was interrupted either way (T-2857).
- The pane and band wear the forge palette: fire-gold for what runs, the blue flame for reading, the site's status colours (T-2853).
- The moonshot reviewer scores each moon's wildness (1-5); an extreme or massive moon below 4 counts as xhigh, the generation brief carries six wild lenses and the reviewer's anti-patterns, and 38 new moons (M-21..M-58) join the catalogue, 19 of them at wild 4+: regrow half a codebase, boot an app that traffic compiles into existence, turn a live Python server into Rust mid-session (T-2858). `sampo moons check` runs the catalogue's checks alone.
- `sampo outcomes --questions [--since TS] [--none]` counts AskUserQuestion calls by outcome; with `--none` it fails when any was asked, and a steer rule may use that read-only form as its `--check` (T-2840).
- An agent resumed with SendMessage counts as running background work, so drive waits for its round instead of pushing the next task (T-2848).
- Every reader of typed commands and goals (cost, repeats, friction, bench, long runs, mandate, Rune, fixes, the CLI, the Smith TUI, Louhi) counts `sampo` as it counts `fm`; the rename scan covers every module and judges each pattern by what it matches (T-2845).
- `fm` or `sampo` run inside a builder lane re-runs that lane's own plugin, once, and only for a lane the main repo's worktree registry names back that you own; checks and writes then hit the lane, not the main install (T-2843).
- Skills, commands, agents, the rules and the docs teach `sampo` spellings (`sampo task new`, `sampo check` …), and README and INSTALL say `fm` still works; every hook, core and guard pattern that matched an `fm` command matches `sampo` too, and a ratchet counts the `fm` spellings left (T-2778).
- `fm code` (Smith) starts a run on the `--escalate` model when its own settled closes of that type and tier keep needing later fixes (5 or more settled, 30% or more fixed later or reverted); each close records the harness that made it (T-2739).
- Stack cards for junit:junit, knip, mime-guess, Kotlin Android, org.json, the JUnit platform launcher, Robolectric and psutil (T-2799).
- `fm doctor`'s hook SLO judges only PreToolUse runs taken at normal host load: each timing records load per CPU, and runs on a busy machine are left out and counted in the row (T-2798).
- A test can name the files it guards on a `# map-links: PATH …` line, so `fm check --affected` runs a test that reads other modules as text when they change (T-2838).
- A criterion whose text quotes the `— verify with` form runs its own trailing command, not the quoted one (T-2839).
- The Stop drive counts a Bash call the harness moved to the background and a Monitor as running work, and with no task active names the next task once per running set instead of on every stop (T-2790).
- One parser reads typed verify commands: `TEXT :: CMD` (split on the last ` :: `) and `TEXT — verify with `+"`CMD`"+` work in `task new --ac`, `task ac add/edit`, `fm batch` and `task set --section` alike (T-2789).
- `fm check --affected` hands a bare-name test runner only the tests in its own folder, so a template's `test_*.py` no longer fails every close that touches it (T-2836).
- The rename scan now covers install.sh, the CI workflows, the project templates and the site scripts; their prose says Sampo (T-2818).
- Model-written code that exam and long runs execute no longer inherits the session's Sampo state paths, git variables or load gate (T-2835).
- `fm new` scaffolds a project without waiting on host load: its first `fm check` is a fixed self-test, not a gate competing for the machine (T-2834).
- Sandboxed `fm` runs (demo, drill, exam, bench, long runs, doc fences) no longer wait on or get refused by the host load gate; `fm new`'s first check no longer times out under load in tests (T-2822)
- Links point at the renamed repo: the README, site, install.sh, INSTALL and templates use ChaseSunstrom/sampo and its Pages URL, and the rename scan counts any link to the old address (T-2811)
- Subcommand usage names what was typed (`sampo` or `fm`) on Python 3.12 as well as 3.14 (T-2814)
- The eval cases embed the current rules (T-2812)
- The operating rules and the status line, compact and subagent hook text say Sampo (T-2809)
- Agent, plugin and marketplace descriptions and install.sh's messages say Sampo (T-2808)
- The foreman-ui mod says Sampo on screen: pane, band, toasts and tool text (T-2807)
- MASTER.md names the product Sampo and its parts Smith, Rune, Louhi, Moons, Underworld and the Ward, with the old names as aliases (T-2810)
- Gates under machine load wait or stretch instead of failing: `fm lane brief` holds a new lane past 1.5 load per core, and `fm task finish`, `fm lane merge` and `fm check` wait (bounded) for load to fall and scale their timeouts by load per core (T-2797)
- The close's proof gate, like its scope check, ignores files another lane's merge brought in (T-2791)
- `fm bench duel --ablate` no longer offers `gate-pipe`, which became a guard refusal and is never ablatable (T-2776)
- The guard's no-revise lock is tested under the `sampo` paths the code now lives at, as well as the `fm*` shims (T-2780)
- Hook notes and library messages say Sampo, with Smith, Rune, Louhi and the Ward where a line means one; every reader of a renamed string (the release mark, cost's refusal counter, snapshot subjects) accepts both names, so old logs still count (T-2779)
- The rename ratchet sees old module names passed as keywords, later arguments or f-strings (T-2783)
- `fm task ac ID edit N --text` reads `TEXT :: CMD` or a trailing `— verify with \`CMD\`` as the verify command (T-2786)
- `fm task finish` no longer counts files, or audit staleness, that another task's `fm lane merge` brought in while the task was focused (T-2784)
- `fm doctor`'s module-map check scans the `sampo` package, not the old shims (T-2782)
- Rune's anticipation gaps are consumed: the next generation's predict step lists the last one's missed steers, and sealing its prediction closes those gaps (T-2774)
- A user steer is scored once per cite however many decisions cite it, and the doctor's hint for an unchecked steer names `--cites` (T-2756)
- `fm outcomes --atlas` lists an area only when two distinct tasks there needed a later fix or closed weak (T-2764)
- `fm friction` reports gates slow under machine load as one load line, not as regressions; `check_run` records the load per core (T-2763)
- `fm shot --width W[,W…] --scheme light|dark|both` shoots the page matrix through one browser (T-2758)
- A gate piped into `tail` or `head` without `pipefail` is refused with the fix named, instead of a note that was ignored (T-2765)
- Louhi, Moons and Underworld pages are built from the tower's own records (exam runs, the moonshot catalogue, the not-yet list), with real-number dials and one scene mark per record; the Moons page reads its tiers from the catalogue (T-2767, T-2777)
- The hook's committed-library fallback runs only committed code: an import finder serves every `sampo.*` module and the guard from HEAD, so an uncommitted sibling module can no longer run under the guard (T-2781)
- The library is the `sampo` package (`plugin/lib/sampo/`); every old `fmX` import path stays as a shim (T-2748)
- The skills, commands and output style say Sampo, with Smith, Rune, Louhi and the Ward where a line means one; plugin ids, paths and `fm` command spellings stay (T-2768)
- Tab completion and `fm help --all` teach the Sampo names: completion offers them and walks each to its old verb's subcommands, the shell hook completes `sampo` as well as `fm` (T-2775)
- `sampo` is Foreman's new name, and every old name still works: `sampo smith`, `rune`, `louhi`, `moons`, `underworld` and `ward` run as `code`, `genesis`, `exam`, `genesis moonshot`, `genesis underworld` and `guard`; `fm` says once a day on a terminal that it is now `sampo`; `SAMPO_*` env wins over `FOREMAN_*`; commits say `Sampo-Task` and both trailers are read (T-2766)
- The landing page is in the forge world: the Sampo mill turns over a forge-glow shader while runes write the headline, the stats are gears and dials of Sampo's real totals, a forge strikes beside the Smith section, and one paragraph tells where the names come from. The shader comes to rest within five seconds, runs again under the pointer, and stops offscreen; the page is complete with no JS and under reduced motion.
- README and INSTALL now say Sampo. They carry the anvil-stone header in light and dark (a dawn sky by day, the night with the Otava), the new verbs (`sampo smith`, `rune`, `moons`, `louhi`, `underworld`, `ward`), the Kalevala paragraph that explains the names (once), and an upgrade note listing what keeps working: `fm`, `foreman@foreman`, `/foreman:`, `FOREMAN_*`, `Foreman-Task` and `~/.claude/foreman`.
- The site's charts now use Sampo's palette: bronze, rune blue, ember, verdigris, violet and rose. `design/sampo/tokens.json` holds six chart colours per theme, each at least 3:1 against the chart panel and well apart in hue from its neighbours. The site's chart variables and the design preview are generated from that file, and the old blue is gone.
- The site now uses Sampo's design system. Its colours, type and motion come from `design/sampo/tokens.json` in light and dark, and it has the anvil-stone mark, favicon and share card. The copy now says Sampo throughout, and the install section lists the old names as aliases. At 768 px the nav no longer pushes the theme button off the page.
- New: Sampo's logo, in `design/sampo/logo/`: the anvil-stone, from design direction 12, the illustrated forge. A steel anvil is set in a rune-cut stone bound with a riveted band and a molten seam, with a fire of many colours behind it and a hammer and a brass star over it; it passed its cold design reviews. It comes as a 16 px icon drawn for that size, a simple mark for 32 to 96 px, mono, a wordmark in Bodoni Moda, an animated mark (the hammer strikes twice, the runes kindle, nothing loops, a still under reduced motion) and a light variant, plus a favicon, app and Desktop icons, README headers, a 1200×630 share card and a pane glyph. The fonts are under the SIL Open Font License and outlined to paths (T-2850).
- Every list `fm` cuts short now says how many it left out, and names the flag that shows them all where one exists. Before, 22 of the 41 places `fm` cut a list printed nothing about the rest. A scan test keeps the count of silent cuts at zero.
- The blocked-pile line at session start now names the one action that frees the most blocked tasks, with its count, before the `fm tidy` hint. That action is your yes to an `fm ask`, an open task closing, or `fm task unblock` for tasks whose blockers have all closed. A wait counts when it's in `depends_on` or written in the block reason ("waits for T-…", "depends on T-…"). On this project's 44 blocked tasks it found 5 still blocked on tasks that closed long ago.
- Session start now names the request Foreman guesses you'll make next, for example "You may ask next: FEATURE (guesses hit 52% vs 39% for 'same as last')". It shows only once the guess has beaten that baseline over at least 20 scored rows and isn't muted, never in a sensitive project, and once per guess. Each showing is booked, and `fm outcomes --predictions` reads the guesses you saw apart from the rest ("shown at session start: N scored, hit X%").
- The guard now knows the coming Sampo names before any of them exist. It refuses under `sampo`, `plugin/bin/sampo`, the `sampo.*` modules, `SAMPO_*` variables and the new verbs (`rune`, `louhi`, `ward`, `smith`, `moons`, `underworld`) exactly what it refuses under `fm`, `fm*` modules and `FOREMAN_*`, so the rename opens no way around it. A parity test runs the guard's whole refusal corpus spelled both ways.
- New: `fm migrate [--undo] [--dry-run]`, the move behind the coming rename to Sampo. It moves the Foreman home and its siblings (`foreman.draft`, `foreman.evolve`, `foreman.lanes`) to the `sampo` names once, leaves symlinks at the old paths, and backs up the record first. A hashed manifest lets a rerun change nothing, a crash mid-move resume, and `--undo` restore the old layout byte for byte. Across filesystems it refuses rather than copy. Moving your home is yours to run: inside an agent session it allows only `--dry-run`.
- `fm code` now stops a worker turn at once when the worker states a rate or usage limit and goes quiet. The run blocks, naming the limit and the `--escalate` model to rerun with, instead of waiting out the step's timeout and every fix round. A word like "quota" or "429" in passing still only labels the stall.
- `fm code`'s home now ranks projects by need: open asks or blocked tasks first, then a failed run, then running, then queue age. A line says what changed since you last looked, and `/` plus a name jumps to a project. `-p` takes a unique slug prefix or folder name, and names the matches when one is ambiguous.
- Fixed: `fm fsck` reported Genesis's prediction-score journal as torn, because its writer never ended a row with a newline. `c.append_line` now ends any non-empty text with a newline, so no writer can leave a journal torn that way. `fm fsck --fix` now mends a journal (never the hash-chained ledger) whose last record is whole and lacks only its newline, and says "mended", not "removed".
- The Genesis sweep's lean row no longer turns red on measurement noise. Hook p95 is the median of three bench runs, and the same tree measured 36.2 ms and then 36.5 ms back to back, so a ratchet with no band failed a generation for nothing. A rise now counts only past the spread of the three runs taken at check time. When the runs agree, any rise still fails.
- `fm next`'s ship step now also fires while a Genesis tower is open. With the task queue closed, the tower's stage is always "genesis", never "idle", so the rule below never fired on this project.
- With the queue closed and landed commits that `fm ship` would push, `fm next` now says so. When a user steer in force grants pushes to main and none forbids them, shipping is the required action, with the full path (a ship task, its publish grant, `fm ship`), so the drive ships instead of leaving work local for hours. Without the grant it adds one line with the count. It counts what `fm ship` itself would pick: the commits since the last ship, else `git cherry` against main, so pushes main takes on its own (`fm genesis live`'s site) don't count.
- `fm notify` now records each delivery's result. `fm doctor` warns, naming the command and its last error, once the command has failed three times in a row. Before, a broken notify command failed silently on every alert it was meant to carry.
- The left-out scan now covers every `plugin/lib/fm*.py` module, not only `fmcli.py`. Every on-screen list a module cuts says how many it left out. The "why" text of a proof or readiness need (the UI, TUI, infra, migration, data and meaning checks) does too, through the new `c.joinmore`. A cut that isn't a list carries a mark naming why.
- A Genesis cold read's exam evidence now keeps one line per run, agent and generation. Before, a later generation graded on the same run replaced the earlier generation's line, so the reader refused a true lineage line it could no longer see.
- Genesis lineage lines now say whose grade is whose ("gen 3 closed 2 train vs gen 2's 3"). Before, "train closed 3" named no generation, so when a later generation was graded on the same exam run, the cold read checked the old line against the new grade and refused the file.
- Each question Claude asks you through AskUserQuestion is now a ledger row (`question_answered`). It counts how many answers took the first option (the asker's default), another option or your own words, or that you dismissed the question. Only counts and the short headers are kept, never your answers. Before, approvals were logged but questions never were, so nothing could learn when you override a default (moonshot M-13's need).
- A brainstorm's canary must now be a built feature near the idea (its nouns or module). Before, any term any search finds would do: Mission 7's grounding met the rule with `def main` on 156 of its 169 "new" verdicts, which shows only that the grep ran. `fm research add` warns when one canary term covers more than half of a catalogue's 10 or more canaries.
- New brainstorm lens, `fm ideas --lens grid`. It crosses the request's surfaces with its actors (you, the agent, other agents, an unattended run) and failure modes (missing, wrong, slow, unsafe, unclear), asks for one idea per cell that matters, and names the cells it left empty. The brainstorm skill names it for exhaustive requests.
- The brainstorm skill's grounding step now runs a canary before it accepts a "not found": the same tool and scope must find a term known to be there. Otherwise a search that couldn't find anything reads the same as an idea that isn't built. Each "new" verdict records its canary, so a catalogue can count them.
- Foreman's windowed readers (the survival and calibration windows in `fm outcomes`, and `fm digest`'s days) now read one clock, `c.clock()`. Tests can pin it with `FOREMAN_NOW`, and new tests stand exactly on each window's edge: a close at the end counts, one a second earlier doesn't. Outside tests the variable pins every timestamp Foreman writes, so `fm doctor` warns while it's set.
- `fm doctor`'s state-dir row now warns when Foreman's state directory is on a RAM filesystem (tmpfs or ramfs), wherever it lives: the default, `FOREMAN_STATE` or a fallback. Before, it warned only when a fallback was under the temp dir. Every brief and ledger there is lost at reboot.
- Fixed: the guard could hang on a command with deeply nested `$(`. Every level re-read the rest of the command, so the work grew about n⁴: 20 levels took 19 seconds and 500 never finished. More than 8 nested at once is now refused at once with the guard's existing "nesting too deep" message. A new latency test times ten pathological commands (a 50,000-character line, 200 heredocs, 10,000 `;`-joined commands and others) against CPU-time bars, and a planted slow regex proves the test can fail.
- The steer expander's one-section output stays. A second try at anticipation without harm split the expansion into a Rule (acted on) and Proposals (filed, never acted on), with the judge reading the Rule only. On the train split it covered 15 of 93 reference clauses against 20 before, with 46 harmful additions against 45: the expander moved clauses the user meant into Proposals. It was measured and reverted.
- New: `fm run --stall-usd N` stops starting tasks once the run has spent N dollars since its last task close, and its end line says why. A run that keeps closing tasks is never stopped by it. Like `--max-usd`, it runs one task at a time so each session's cost is measured.
- Fixed: `fm burden` undercounted Foreman's injected context. Claude Code delivers SessionStart's context as `hook_success` stdout, which the count never read. With it, the last 7 days read 76 characters per turn instead of 48. The new `fm burden --by-source` splits that figure by hook, biggest first: SessionStart 42.9, Stop 14.5, PreToolUse 9.0. PostToolUse's rewritten tool output isn't counted, since that's the tool's own output.
- New: `fm prove --steer` runs each unsettled user-steer rule's `[check:]` on the start tree of the task the rule's row names. A check that passes there too doesn't test the rule, so it's listed as vacuous, together with the task it used, and the run exits 1. Rows that name no task or no start snapshot are skipped with the reason. It runs this tree's tests at older commits, so like the other modes that run code it needs `--yes-run` once.
- A capture that matches a deferred task of its type now wakes that task instead of being refused as a near-duplicate. Your words go on the deferred task's log and it's due back today, so `fm next` names it. No second task is made. A match with an open task is still refused as before.
- A close that `fm prove` still lists now says so beside its grade, for example "verification: strong — 2 ran, lenses: edge; unproven: perf-typed". The kinds are kept on the brief and its `task_done` event, and the weekly line no longer counts such a close as strongly verified ("3 strongly verified, 1 closed unproven"). Before, a PERFORMANCE close timed without `--bench`, or one that left docs unchecked, read as strong in every digest.
- Fixed two guard over-refusals. `git apply` with only `--check`, `--stat`, `--numstat` or `--summary` reads and is no longer refused as a tree write. A plain `git apply`, or one that adds `--apply`, `--index` or `--reverse`, still is. A Python heredoc that puts Foreman's `plugin/lib` on `sys.path` and writes a scratch file is no longer refused as writing `plugin/lib`, the shape behind 7 recorded refusals. The same path opened for writing still blocks. A third proposed fix, letting a credential name like `.ssh` through when the code calls no known read API, was built and then reverted: `io.FileIO`, `linecache` and `fileinput` read a file without any listed API, so that refusal stays fail-closed.
- Fixed: a ledger line that a crash cut short as it was written (rather than zero-filled) left `fm verify-ledger` reporting the ledger broken for good, because `--accept-damage` covered only zeroed lines. Once a later line chains to the cut bytes, they are what was written, not an edit, so the line now counts as crash damage that `--accept-damage` records once. A cut newest line, which nothing pins yet, still reads as broken. volt-89d78b's line 24329, cut on 2026-10-23, was the first case and is now accepted.
- New: `fm drill --restore [NAME]` rehearses a host wipe. It lays an upgrade snapshot (the newest by default) into a temp home and passes only when each project's ledger chain verifies, fsck is clean, and `fm state` and `fm outcomes` run without a traceback. Your live state is never touched. Its first run, on the 1.2.50 snapshot, read back 22 of 23 projects and found a ledger torn mid-file on 2026-10-23 that nothing had reported.
- `fm drill` has a seventh fault, a dropped ledger class: every `task_done` line is gone while briefs still say closed. `fm outcomes` and `fm digest` now say the class is missing and the rates built from it are unmeasured, instead of printing a zero rate as if it were measured.
- `fm task evidence --bench CMD --under MS` is now a pass/fail proof. It exits 1 and records the median against the bar when the current tree's median is over MS milliseconds, and passes when it's under. `--under` needs `--bench` and a bar above 0.
- Fixed: a `--bench` run whose command failed was recorded without the failed-run mark, so it read as a pass and checked its step off. It's now a failed run that the step and the finish gate both see.
- Every nonzero `fm` exit now ends with one `Next:` command. A refusal names `fm next`, a lock timeout repeats the same command, and corrupt or unreadable state names `fm fsck`. Exit codes are unchanged.
- A contract's receipt now shows yield per dollar beside spend, for example "$0.50 a task (2.0 tasks a dollar)". `fm run` ends with the same line when it measured spend, which is under `--max-usd` or a USD contract. Zero spend or zero tasks show no ratio.
- The brainstorm skill's grounding step now asks for MASTER.md first, three phrasings per idea and the verbs' own help strings before an idea counts as new, plus a spot check of two grounded items before any is built. In the 2 November run, three of the four items checked that the first pass had marked new or partial were already built.
- `fm task finish` no longer re-runs a criterion whose exact command just passed on the same files and environment. If the newest run of that command was a pass recorded by fm on this tree and world, the close reuses it and says so ("Reused 1 run(s)…"). A different tree, a changed lockfile or interpreter, or a later failed run of the command still runs it again. The close's own gates still run.
- A removed `fm` flag now says what replaced it and which task removed it, instead of argparse's bare error. The first one is `fm night --print-timer`, removed in T-2475, whose replacements are `fm night --stage` and `fm night --install`. It exits 2.
- `flakes.json` now counts each flaky test's fails and passes, and `fm doctor` lists the three worst by fail rate, with the command that makes each one flake.
- `fm ship` refuses to push when the tree it would push isn't the tree its tests ran on, for example when a test command committed in the scratch worktree. It names both tree hashes and pushes nothing.
- `fm doctor` checks, at most once a day, that each model name Foreman pins still answers a one-line call: the review models, the moonshot rater, and the exam and steer defaults. The call runs through Foreman's isolated child runner. If one fails, it WARNs with the model and the modules that pin it, and stays quiet when `claude` isn't installed.
- Fixed: the guard now treats an unquoted argument on a `python3 - ARG <<EOF` line as a path the script may write, as it already did for a quoted one. `python3 - ~/.claude/settings.json <<EOF` with `open(sys.argv[1], 'w')` used to write a protected file without a check.
- An edit that adds a top-level def, class or assignment its Python module already defines now gets a note at once, naming the name and both lines. Before, a second `def` silently replaced the first, and only the full test suite caught it (three times so far, most recently a helper in the guard that broke six guard tests). Decorated repeats such as `@overload` stubs and `@f.register` handlers are left alone.
- `fm exam steer` now always uses the strongest model as its judge, so runs with different expanders can be compared. Tuning the expander's method prompt on the train split didn't help. Four variants were tried, the last round under one judge, and asking for more anticipation added about 50% more harmful clauses while covered clauses stayed at 19 to 21 of 93, within judge noise. The original prompt stays. The next idea, splitting the rule from proposals so anticipated extras aren't acted on, is T-2632.
- Fixed: the guard read `2>/dev/null` as an argument. Commands like `cp a b 2>/dev/null` (or `2>&1`) were refused as writes to a file named `2`, and the real destination went unchecked. Bash treats the digits glued to a redirect as its file descriptor, and the guard now does too. Fixed: a Python heredoc that used `"\n".join(out)` was refused as a write to `.join(out)+`, because the quote scan paired the closing quote of one string with the opening quote of the next. For Python that parses, the guard now reads the code's real string literals, plus any quoted path inside one. A path literal is always a real string, so nothing it caught before is lost.
- Genesis now advances the moonshot campaign (MOONSHOT.md part A) in every generation instead of saving it for after the run, per your steer to integrate it into each generation. One catalogue, `moonshot.md`, grows across generations toward 100 moonshots on the six-tier mix (5 low, 10 medium, 15 high, 20 xhigh, 25 extreme, 25 massive). Each seed's Moonshot section shows the catalogue against the mix, what this generation adds, kill tests for every extreme and massive idea, the next climb stages, and the steer exam's held-out rate. `fm genesis moonshot rate` has the strongest model tier each new moonshot without seeing the author's tier; a tier typed into the file doesn't count, and demotions are logged. `fm genesis moonshot order` is the climb: first the smaller moonshots that unblock the most big ones, then big ones whose needs are built, staged kill test, smallest slice, full build. `fm genesis check` holds the catalogue to the mix and the required lines, rejects duplicates of other moonshots or of built work, and requires kill results and measures. It also enforces a floor: from generation 3, each generation must advance an extreme or massive moonshot by one stage with a finished task. The Genesis page lists the catalogue, both tiers, and which moonshots you never asked for. Generation 3 was already running, so its moonshot Mission gets its instructions from `fm genesis moonshot`. Generations 1 and 2 had finished and stay as they were filed. From generation 4, a seed without the campaign is flagged.
- The guard's "interpreter code driving Foreman's modules" refusal now says which of its six clauses fired. It names Foreman's modules showing up in shell text it can't read as Python, a PYTHON setting, code that doesn't parse, dynamic code or an unusual import (with the name), an unknown run folder or a file there shadowing an import, or a direct import of an fm module (with the module). The rule id and its hint are unchanged. It fired 42 times in the week to 1 November, and each time the rewrite was a guess.
- New: `fm exam steer`, the steer-expansion exam (MOONSHOT.md part B). It pairs a user's raw words with the standing rule they became: `fm exam steer cases --from MOONSHOT.md` reads part C's 19 pairs, and every brief whose raw ask a later logged user steer corrected adds one more (43 cases here, 13 held out by the exam's own hash). `fm exam steer run` expands each case from the raw words alone (Sonnet by default). A judge one model rank stronger (Opus) marks which reference clauses are covered and which additions would do harm, and a verdict counts only when it quotes the expansion's own words. Live, each instruction-shaped message is expanded in the background (never in a sensitive project, at most 40 a day, `fm exam steer live off`). A standing rule becomes a queued task carrying the held-out score; under 70% it is also listed for your review (`fm decide --review`). A `steer:` correction on that task becomes a new case. The newest run's held-out rate is in genesis.json and on the Genesis page's exam section. The first real run is the baseline: 19% of held-out clauses covered (6% on train), with 48 additions the judge called harmful, such as a USD cap added after "no token budget". Raising it is T-2627, tuned on the train split only.
- The daily session review no longer turns pasted credentials or settled asks into inbox items. A line like "try these: KEY=…" became a confirm-first task (redacted, but still noise), and an ask a closed task had already settled (a flag deliberately removed, say) read as unanswered because the reviewer saw only open work. It now skips any ask holding a redacted credential, shows the reviewer the 40 most recently closed tasks, and tells it to ignore credentials and pasted or quoted text.
- The docs proof kind no longer counts what `fm prove --docs` can't check. History files (CHANGELOG, CHANGES, HISTORY, NEWS) are point-in-time records that `fm prove --docs` skips by design (T-1817), yet the kind flagged tasks for the commands in them, and the green run that cleared the flag never read those lines. It now skips them too. It also reads only the doc lines a task itself landed: the diff stops at the task's own newest commit (the one whose subject starts with its id) instead of HEAD, so a later task's lines no longer count against an earlier one. The cached rollup is computed again after a rule change. On Foreman's own record, docs items fell from 601 to 421; the rest are real `--flag` claims in MASTER.md and other docs that no `fm prove --docs` run has checked (T-2616).
- `fm exam long grade` now splits false dones in two. A failed hidden check that names a missing attribute, key or import the goal text never mentions is out of the goal's reach; anything else is a real miss. The grade prints, for example, "false done 6 (2 only on names the goal never states)", and its JSON carries each failed check's line and class. Regrading the kept runs costs no tokens. l001's foreman arm now reads 2 of its 6 false dones as out of reach and l002's as 0 of 6. At the check level that is 13 of 31 failed checks, close to the 17 a hand read found. Return-shape failures such as "object is not subscriptable" stay counted as real, so the split is a lower bound (T-2613).
- `fm ship --chance N` now names the ship record when your number is far from it. Once 20 or more ship bets have settled, a chance 15+ points from their held share prints one line after the bet, for example "Ship record: 21 of 21 ship bets held (100%); 85% sits 15 points under it". The outside signal used to reach only the weekly digest's trails line; now it shows where the next number is typed (T-2611).
- The track record and the calibration ledger now compare like with like. A miss used to count the day it landed, but a hold only once its 30-day window ended, so every read made the record look worse than it was: the track record shown at `fm focus` said FEATURE M held 21 of 43 when finished windows held 95%. Now a close, and a task or choice bet on it, counts only once its window has finished. This applies to the track record, the ledger's buckets, both Briers, the trails clause, the research bar check and the base rate. A loss inside an open window shows on its own line until the window ends, and the interim read counts it as not held so far. A Brier trial on the research bar stays pending while the bets placed since the move are still inside their windows, instead of proposing a revert for "no scored bets" (T-2610).
- The watch exam kind now has blind variants. In about a third of scenarios the setup gives no watch yes for issues, so the tick never sees them. Such a scenario passes only if no work starts from those issues and the tick reports issues as blind (via T-2607's report), so the exam checks that a quiet tick is honest about what it can't see.
- The night tick now says what it couldn't watch. `fm doctor`'s night-timers row names each trigger kind it skipped (CI, issues) with the reason: no watch yes (and the command that asks for one), a sensitive project, or `FOREMAN_NO_NET`. With a tick timer installed, the session note mentions it for a few days after the list changes. A quiet night now means quiet, not blind.
- `fm task ac ID edit N --verify CMD` now works on a criterion whose recorded runs all matched no test, for example a misspelled test module. Such a run measured nothing, so fixing its command weakens no check. A criterion with any real run, passing or failing, still keeps its check.
- The self-change gate catches a hook that waits (a sleep, slow I/O) on a busy machine more reliably. When the machine is loaded it now compares the hooks' median wall time rather than their p95, which load spikes inflate. This also fixes a test that failed now and then under the full suite.
- `fm exam runs` lists every exam run, newest first: its tier, how many scenarios it has and how many are held out, and for each agent that graded it, the generation, model and date of its newest grade. The "no exam run" error now points to it.
- The Genesis sweep counts a Mission task deferred with a revisit date as carried, not as open work, the same way the build loop already does. Its line says how many are carried.
- `fm scout --slow` and `fm lib` no longer list a path dependency, such as a Cargo workspace crate (`logic = { path = "logic" }`), as a stack that needs a card: it's the project's own code.
- `fm release --push` now lands on a main that `fm ship` has been feeding by cherry-pick. When the release merge conflicts only because the histories split, it commits the release with main's CI-ignored data laid over it. That happens only if every other file main differs in holds a version the branch once had; a real change on main still stops it. Afterwards the branch moves to the landed release. And `fm release VERSION` continues from an unpushed release commit even after `--abandon`. 1.2.51 had to be landed by hand for both reasons.
- `fm doctor` warns when the CHANGELOG's Unreleased section has gone 14 days or 150 entries without a release, and names `fm release`. 1.2.51 had waited 12 days with 746 entries before anyone noticed.

## 1.2.51 — 2026-11-02
### Highlights

- **Genesis.** `fm genesis` runs self-directed generations: a seed, Missions with exams, critiques, a drift check, a cross-cutting review on a stronger model, then selection. The tower is published as a live page (`fm genesis live`).
- **Proof by kind.** `fm prove --docs`, `--infra`, `--data` and `--release` check claims on throwaway copies. Every result ends with a "Does not prove:" line naming what it didn't cover.
- **Goals that finish.** `fm goal` tracks a goal's items. A done-when counts only if it fails on the item's start tree, and each line of a goal's "Done when:" list needs a check that names it.
- **Leads, accounted for.** `fm find` ranks what needs doing (`--ladder`). A session must fix, report or refute every lead it was shown before its turn ends.
- **Safer shipping.** `fm ship` re-picks over the tower's data publishes and retries a push race. It never pushes a tree that wasn't tested with what it ships. `fm ci-watch --last-ship` watches the commit that ship actually pushed.
- **Exams.** `fm exam` grades Foreman on held-out scenarios across 13 capabilities, including a long-horizon tier. `fm exam scorecard` shows where each capability stands.
- **Night work.** `fm night` does budgeted background work while you're away and skips cleanly when usage is high. `fm doctor` now says when an installed timer stopped firing or failed.
- **Doctor.** New rows cover the state size, with `fm tidy --apply` now pruning old exam work copies, and permission prompts you approve by reflex (advisory: Foreman never adds a rule). A failed doctor run is recorded by the check that failed.
- **Design and 3D.** `fm design` imports Figma and DTCG tokens, lints and x-rays a page; `fm scene` builds 3D scenes from data; `fm motion` compiles a motion score.
- **Coding harness.** `fm code` runs a task with any configured model backend and lists what it couldn't verify.

### Everything else

- The note that opens each session now fits 1,500 characters, the same limit Foreman's friction report holds notes to. The standing mandate or goal and the starting leads now count against that limit; before, they were added after the budget and pushed the note to about 2,000 characters. The recap after a compaction and the repo note are still added on purpose, and the friction report no longer counts them against the note.
- `fm task finish` now reruns a step whose `fm check --affected` evidence failed by its test command. Before, it tried to run the evidence label itself and failed with a shell syntax error.
- `fm doctor`'s "reflex prompts" row now says no permission prompts have been recorded yet, and why: bypass mode shows none. Before, it said "none qualified", which read like a finding.
- `fm ship` now skips the retest after main moves mid-ship only when every file main gained is one your CI workflow ignores (its `paths-ignore` list), such as the Genesis tower's data publishes. A move in any other file stops the ship with "run fm ship again", and the next ship tests the new main. Before, a code change in a file the shipped commits didn't touch could be pushed on top without ever being tested together with them. A repo with no `paths-ignore` refuses every such move.
- Three numbers that read wrong now read right (from the gen-2 review). The model-versus-rule exam line says "model not run (no night answers booked)" when no model has answered yet, instead of scoring the model 0/0. A review finding can rest on a short number quoted with its words, such as "landed 57%"; a bare "57%" still can't. And the survival line and track record now count a close as held or lost only once its 30-day window has ended, so recent closes no longer inflate "held". The loss rate is taken over closes whose window is over.
- `fm night` now exits 0 when it skips a night because usage is high. It still prints "nothing runs tonight". systemd used to record each skipped night as a failed run, so `fm doctor` and the session-start watch reported your night timer as failing when it was working as designed.
- `fm genesis review N --handle K=HOW` can now be given several times in one call, and every value is applied. Before, only the last one counted and the rest were dropped without a word. Every value is checked first, so one bad value applies none.
- `fm loop round --next` no longer ends a round that started under 15 minutes ago and has made no task yet. It prints that round's instructions again. Before, a session that called it a few times to re-read the instructions ended two empty rounds as dry, and that switched the forever loop off. `fm loop yield N` still closes a round at any time.
- `fm tidy --apply` now removes the work copies of old exam runs. These are the repo copies that sessions worked in, and they took 2.3 GB of state here. A run qualifies once its newest grade is 14 days old and the same agent has had a newer run of the same tier graded since. Answers, grades and keys stay, and runs that were never graded are left alone. Grading a pruned run now refuses and names the rerun command, instead of failing every goal for want of its files.
- A failed `fm doctor` run is now recorded by the check that failed, not by its summary line. `fm digest`'s "Recurring failures" used to show "fm doctor: failed (0 pass, 0 warn, 0 fail)" over and over (numbers are zeroed in signatures), which named no check. `fm doctor` also prints its FAIL rows last, right above the verdict.
- `fm genesis select` and `fm genesis confirm` no longer count an exam line answered only on the held-out split. Such a line closes no train scenario by construction, so a 0-to-0 tie between two of them would have reverted a generation on an empty comparison. A generation needs a line on the train split (`fm exam run RUN --split train`). When its parent has none, the generation becomes the baseline.
- `fm doctor` has a new "reflex prompts" row. Foreman now times each permission prompt, from the dialog to the call running, minus the tool's own run time. A command class you approved within 2 seconds in 5 or more prompts is named with the allow rule that would stop asking, for example `Bash(git status *)`. `fm digest` lists the same classes when any qualify. Only classes whose every prompt only read, and that you never refused, are ever named. Foreman never adds the rule; you decide. Timings are kept in state/anergy.jsonl, capped at 4,000 rows.
- CI on macOS now runs the test suite in three parts instead of two. At 5,657 tests, half the suite ran past macOS's 1,980-second limit, and the classes still running were killed. That job doesn't block a release, but now each third fits. Ubuntu still runs in halves. A new test pins this layout.
- `fm ideas --seen-latest N` reads the newest N earlier brainstorms' ideas, as if each had been passed with `--seen`. You no longer type the paths, and a run that left no ideas is skipped. The Genesis step's brainstorm line now uses `--seen-latest 6`. Twice in one session, a typed path to a run that didn't exist stopped the brainstorm.
- A goal can no longer read as finished while a line from its "Done when:" list has no real check behind it. Each line now needs an item whose done-when command names it, with at least two of the line's words; sharing words with an item's description no longer counts. On Foreman's long-horizon exam, sessions claimed done with stated requirements no check tested. One built the feature but wrote no test for any of its three Done-when lines.
- `fm ship` no longer fails when another writer, such as `fm genesis live`'s tower publish, lands on main between the ship's last check and its push. It fetches main again, re-applies the commits if the move touched none of the shipped files, and pushes again, up to three tries, without rerunning the tests. A move in a shipped file still stops the ship. Before, the rejected push cost a full rerun of the test suite.
- The next-request predictor now learns only from requests you actually made. Two kinds of task are left out: the Genesis tower's own Mission briefs, which `fm intake` used to file as yours, and captures dropped as a mistake. `fm intake --source genesis` files the tower's briefs under their own source, and the Genesis step's intake line now uses it. On the same 331 held-out requests, the cleaned history predicts the request type exactly as often as before (201 hits each) and predicts the files slightly better (overlap 0.118 against 0.113). Against the exam's naive baselines (0.116 for the cleaned history, 0.113 for the old one), that's a gain of 0.002, within noise. The exam's foresight report shows both histories.
- A new test fails when a module in plugin/lib defines the same top-level name twice. In Python the later definition quietly replaces the earlier one, and twice in one session a new helper was shadowed this way. A reassignment that reads the old value (such as compiling a table of patterns in place) is still allowed.
- The MASTER.md matcher from an earlier change in this release, and its `fm recall --backtest-built` command, are removed. Its backtest found no setting worth shipping: at best it caught 4 of 46 already-built tasks while wrongly flagging 31 of 400 new ones. Nothing used it. The result and how to restore the code are kept in the project's research note covered-backtest.
- `fm genesis put` now refuses a Mission file whose intake block has more briefs than grounding lines. Each brief needs a bullet under `## Grounding` naming the code it rests on (a file, an `fm` verb or a module.function). The check runs before the cold read, so an ungrounded Mission costs no model call. Gen-2's Mission 3 filed five briefs, and three turned out to be built already or to fix a failure that had never happened.
- The exam's tool-build and tool-find grading no longer marks a correct tool as failed when its file is briefly busy. Grading runs on several threads, and running a file that another thread's process still has open for writing fails with "text file busy". That exec is now retried, which removes a flaky failure in the full test run.
- Foreman now notices when an installed `fm night` timer stops firing or its runs fail. It reads systemd's own record of each timer's last trigger and each run's result. `fm doctor` names the timer and the `journalctl` command to read; with nothing wrong, it says how many timers are firing. The Stop hook's background sweep caches the finding, so the next session's Watch line shows it with no one asking. `fm night --install` now runs the tick once and reports how that first run went.
- `fm task step ID remove 2` (or `delete`, or `del`) now removes step 2. Before, it added a step named "remove" and quietly dropped the number. Two bare words where step text belongs, such as `fm task step ID fix it`, are now refused rather than half-added; quote the text instead.
- `fm genesis step` no longer stalls a generation on a Mission task that was deferred with a revisit date (`fm task defer --until`). The task is carried: the critique step names it and asks why it waits. A task deferred without a date, or still planned, still holds the build stage.
- The usage-burn advice in `fm cost` and `fm doctor` no longer tells you to run `fm install-user` when your own settings already set a larger compact window. That command leaves your value alone, so the advice did nothing. It now names your window and suggests running /compact at task boundaries. The doctor's state-size check now says when most of the space is exam runs, because `fm tidy` doesn't prune those.
- `fm bench duel --ablate` now files a cleanup candidate for each hook feature that, when switched off, passed as many cases as full Foreman at no more cost. The candidate names the run and says what a cut still needs: the feature switched off must score no lower on every exam kind (held-out included) and on the long-horizon tier. Running it again doesn't file the same candidate twice.
- If an edit to Foreman's own code breaks its library so it can't be imported, the SessionStart, Stop, UserPromptSubmit and other non-tool hooks now run from the last committed copy, as the guard already did. Before, they quietly did nothing, so the drive, the done-gate and the session context switched off without a trace. Each fallback is logged to hooks.log with the event's name, so `fm doctor` shows it.
- The exam no longer draws its package kind on a host whose Python has no setuptools (for example, a uv-managed Python), because nothing could build the package there. Before, that item was drawn anyway and always failed.
- `fm ci-watch --last-ship` watches the main commit that the newest `fm ship` pushed (`fm ship` now records it in `refs/foreman/shipped-tip`). An empty `--commit` (for example, a sha extraction that found nothing) is now refused instead of quietly watching the branch's HEAD.
- The exam has a new Python-only kind for capability #10 (beyond code): make the repo an installable package. It's graded by installing the package with no network into a throwaway environment and running its `app-total` command. It's a kind of its own, so no existing exam item moved.
- `fm pr train` now runs the self-change gate on each pull request before merging it, as `fm lane merge` and `fm serve` already do. A PR that changes Foreman's own `plugin/` and would weaken the guard, slow a hook or grow the footprint stops the train; your yes to `fm ask ID core` lets it through.
- `fm task finish` now refuses to close, and commits nothing, while the newest full `fm check` on the same working tree failed. It names the failing gate and tests. A newer passing run on that tree lets the close go ahead, and so does any edit since, which changes the tree; the close's own checks then judge.
- A task always gets a start point, so `fm task finish --commit` can tell its files apart. A git lookup that times out at focus is retried with a longer wait, and a timeout at finish no longer reads as "no start point". A task that still has none gets HEAD at its first evidence, with a log line saying so.
- `fm goal plan`'s checklist of past misses now also names finished tasks that a later fix or a revert touched (for example "T-X was fixed later by T-Y"), from `fm outcomes`. Before, it only saw tasks reopened within 7 days, which missed every follow-up fix filed as new work.
- `fm ci-watch` files one task per failing cause, not one per run. It names the cause by the failing test classes in the log, which stay the same across shards and commits. A repeat while that task is open is logged on it, and the same failure after the task closed files a new one. Blocking failures now join their open task too, as non-blocking ones already did; runs whose log names nothing still file one task each.
- When the cold rival reads a request differently from the plan, the door gate now treats the task as ambiguous. A one-way step in it parks for you instead of running as a costly decision, even if the request names the step. Before, that signal was recorded but nothing read it.
- The base rate that stated confidences are compared against now counts "held" the same way bets are scored: only a revert or a later fix (or a failed first finish) is a miss. A correction or a smoke-test failure used to count against the base rate but not against the bet, which made the base rate look worse than it was.
- The night tick now records how many bets it settled: its printed line says "N bet(s) settled", and the ledger event it writes carries the count, so settling with no session can be checked rather than assumed.
- Foreman's known-fix store no longer learns a no-op as a fix. A failed `mytool --check` followed by `touch mytool` (or `mkdir`, `test`, `true`, `:`) used to be stored as "run `touch mytool` instead"; now only a real retry pairs.
- `fm outcomes --judgment` now shows ship bets in its buckets. They were scored but missing from the table; on this repo, 4 of 4 have held. They still never move a bar or a route.
- Each bet now records which harness stated it: fm code, the exam, or a Claude Code session. A harness it can't recognize reads "unknown" rather than a guess. `fm outcomes --judgment --by harness` shows the buckets and Brier scores for each harness.
- Bets now settle even on nights when no task closes: fm night's tick writes the outcomes that are due. Once 20 bets are scored, `fm digest` names the kind of bet whose stated confidence scores worst against its base rate (or says none does).
- `fm lane brief` now says when a task's own sections run past 4,000 characters, quotes the landing rates from `fm lane stats` (on this repo, briefs of 8–16k landed 26 of 46 times, 57%, against 378 of 386, 98%, for shorter ones), and suggests splitting the task or keeping it in the main thread. It still writes the brief.
- Each check fm runs as proof now records the world it ran in: the Python version and the repo's lockfiles. `fm doctor` names finished tasks from the last 30 days whose world has changed since (for example, "uv.lock changed"), so you know which passes to rerun.
- Every `fm prove` result now ends with a "Does not prove:" line for its mode, so a pass isn't read as more than it is. For example, `--docs` says only that each documented command, flag or path exists or ran, not that the prose is right; a scan says that leads weren't tried and that a proven ticket shows a failing test, not its cause.
- Each claim in a research note now says how it was learned: `[read]` from a cited page, `[ran]` from a command, or `[recalled]` with no source. `fm prove` already flags a research Decision with no verified claim, and its message now says the Decision rests on recalled or unchecked claims only.
- `fm prove` now catches unproven terminal UI changes too: a TUI screen, a statusline or a mod's hooks changed with no recorded run that renders it is listed as `tui-unshot`. A run naming the file clears it, as does `claude plugin test` for a mod's hooks; an `echo` that only names the file does not.
- `fm friction` now lists the fm verbs and flags that sessions reached for but don't exist (such as `fm next --last`), ranked by how many sessions tried each one, as votes for what to add.
- `fm lane stats` shows how often builder lanes landed, by the size of the brief each builder got (up to 4k, 8k, 16k characters, or more). `fm lane brief` now records each brief's size; older briefs are sized from their file when it's still on disk. On this repo, briefs up to 4k characters landed 25 of 26 times (96%), up to 8k 353 of 360 (98%), and briefs of 8–16k 26 of 46 (57%).
- `fm outcomes --judgment` adds an interim read. Survival takes 30 days to score a task, so the read shows, per stated-confidence bucket, the bets on tasks closed 7 or more days ago and how many have held so far.
