It is labeled as never scored and moves no bar. The MCP `calibration` tool returns it, and the site's Judgment page plots the share held per bucket, with interim rows marked.
- While background work runs, the Stop drive offers each stage of a side task once per session: planning a captured task, then drafting a planned one. Before, every change of active task started the offers over, so the same draft was asked for again.
- On macOS, Foreman now recognizes Claude Code installed through npm. macOS names a script's process after its interpreter (`node`, `sh`), so Foreman used to miss it. The lanes drill's stand-in for Claude, a shell script, is found the same way, which fixes the macOS CI failure "builder process is not recorded".
- The install and permission requests waiting on you are now listed by how much open work each yes frees: the task itself plus every open task waiting on it, directly or through others. On a tie the oldest comes first. `fm todo`, the waiting-requests line in `fm next` and the Stop hook, and the MCP list all use this order.
- `fm ship` no longer gives up when main moves during its test run in files the shipped commits never touch, such as Genesis's own tower publishes. It re-picks onto the new main and pushes. A move in a shipped file still refuses.
- When a plan's Choice percent sits 20 or more points from how often that type and size of task has held up here, `fm task set`, `fm focus` and `fm next` say so, naming both numbers. It is a note, never a refusal.
- Ship bets: `fm ship --chance N` states the chance that CI's blocking jobs pass on the pushed commit. `fm ci-watch` settles it from the run, so a stated number is scored within hours instead of after 30 days. Ship bets are their own kind in `fm outcomes --judgment` and never move a bar.
- Two macOS-only test failures are fixed. The infrastructure-proof test now expects the real path of a symlinked temp folder, which every macOS one is. On hosts without `/proc`, a process's start time gets one patient retry when `ps` is slow under load, instead of the process being treated as gone.
- Each Genesis generation seals a prediction of your next three steers before they arrive, and every steer you send is scored against it. A miss is filed as an anticipation gap. Before the next Seed, Foreman hunts blind spots from inputs it didn't write: session replays, your past steers grouped into classes, failures nobody filed, inversion, and one far-domain comparison. Every class it finds becomes a rule with a passing check, and the sweep checks the seal, the hunt and the hit rate.
- Proof before presentation: the Genesis sweep reads the README and the site for results (counts, percentages, ratios, comparisons). Each must name a record the sweep can resolve: an exam run, a task that recorded its measurement, or live data. A line saying a capability passes must match the scorecard. The two older static results on the site now cite the tasks that measured them, and rule descriptions are marked as rules.
- The Genesis sweep's lean check measures substance: code and test lines per module, and dependencies, against the generation's start. Each Mission's growth is judged by its gain. Growth whose tasks held up passes, growth not yet measured waits, and growth that was measured with no gain turns the sweep red and files a task to subtract it.
- Genesis redraws its gap map every generation against the current code, after the cross-cutting review. The next Seed ranks Missions by expected value: the gap map's tier, the exam scorecard, how the area's last tasks survived, and the review's gaps. Empty coverage cells only break ties. Each Seed prints every Mission's score parts. The sweep goes red when the map is missing, older than the review, or skips a capability.
- While background work runs, the Stop drive offers each queued task once per active task. A change in which background jobs are running no longer offers the same tasks again.
- The model layer can use a server you own. Mark its `models.json` profile `"own": true` and name its key's variable. It is asked after local servers and before cloud ones, and the night shadow pass can use it, but never for a sensitive project. `TYPESAFE_BASE_URL` adds a Jev-compatible server from the environment. `models.json` is now gitignored.
- Genesis runs a cross-cutting review each generation, after the Mission critiques and before the sweep. A cold reader on a stronger model than the Missions used sees only the generation's diff, exam lines, outcomes and friction. It answers what got worse, what grew without earning it, what is measured wrong, what is presented ahead of its proof, and what a demanding user would complain about first. Findings with no file or number from those inputs are dropped. Each kept one becomes a task, a top gap in the next Seed, or a dismissal with its reason, and the sweep stays red until every one is handled.
- Past 80% weekly usage, while a gate runs in the background, the Stop drive offers a side task as prep in the scratchpad instead of a builder lane, since economy mode allows no subagents.
- `fm doctor` has a self-fixes line: how many of the self-fix trials that closed in the last 7 days held and how many did not, or none. It warns only when one did not hold.
- Model layer surfaces: other agents can ask the model layer through a `decide` MCP tool (booked apart from Foreman's own uses), `fm doctor` lists the model backends it can reach and which uses are live, the AGENTS.md block says a model's answer advises and a run decides, and the site has a Model layer page with the newest exam replay.
- The exam scores the model layer's uses against the rules they would replace. On your own closed tasks it compares how often the rule and the model got the type, the tier and duplicates right, with the model's calibration and latency, and costs no tokens.
- The model layer's first uses run in shadow mode. Capture, intake, the near-duplicate check and `fm find --rank` record the decision their rules made. The hourly tick has a local model answer the same questions beside them, so the exam can compare the two before any use goes live. Nothing is spent without a local model.
- `fm decide` warns when it records a rule from a user steer with no `--check`, naming how to add one, instead of leaving the gap for the doctor to find later. A mistyped flag's "did you mean" now suggests only the typed command's own flags.
- The model layer's rules are checked: no guard or evidence gate can call a model; the layer downloads and installs nothing itself; and a model use goes live only through a decision that cites the exam run it won (`model-live: USE`), staying in shadow mode otherwise.
- The model layer's decision interface: one call picks among choices with a probability. It tries a deterministic rule first, then local models, then cloud ones, never a cloud model for a sensitive project, and books every answer so its calibration can be scored.
- `fm ci-watch` no longer files a new FIX brief on every ship for the same failing non-blocking jobs: the run is logged on the open or blocked brief already filed for them, and a new brief is filed only once that one is closed.
- Genesis's not-yet ledger can hold candidates to evaluate: an item whose blocker says unevaluated owes no tries until an exam runs it. Gen 2 opens with a Model layer Mission, the layer has its own coverage area, and selfjev-4b, jeff, Jevlike and Von wait in the ledger as candidates.
- A Genesis seed now ranks the §2 capabilities ahead of other areas and writes its own order of work, with independent Missions in parallel lanes. When its read fails it keeps the draft so it can be revised.
- The Genesis tower advances itself: a red sweep files one fix task per red line and runs again once they close, and a clean sweep writes the next generation's Seed right away.
- A Genesis generation no longer fails on empty coverage cells: it fails only on templated, duplicated or ungrounded cells, and its empty cells go to the next Seed to fill first. The whole run ends only when no cell is empty.
- Genesis's cold read of each tower file now settles: Haiku reads first and Sonnet decides any file Haiku doesn't pass. A FAIL counts only when it quotes the file's line and names the gen-0 MUST/NEVER or steer it breaks; anything else is a note and the file is filed. A text that was judged keeps its verdict and isn't read again, and Haiku is dropped once Sonnet overrules more than a third of its FAILs.
- Six verbs moved into the verb that already does their job: `fm proof` is `fm prove --rollup`, `fm calibration` is `fm outcomes --judgment`, `fm incident` is `fm capture --incident`, `fm sweep` is `fm projects --sweep`, `fm instruments` is `fm which --instruments` and `fm suspects` is `fm whyred --suspects`. Typing an old name says where it went and runs nothing. The ten longest skill and agent descriptions got shorter, so gen 1 adds no verbs and no always-on text over the seed.
- A race that ties on the evidence now goes against the side whose change holds something you said never to do; with nothing recorded, the stated choice keeps the tie as before.
- A Decisions page on the site: every decision Foreman made on one scrollable timeline, with how sure it was, whether it was a door, and the later decision that reversed it.
- The finish receipt groups the plan's assumptions by how each was checked: ran, read, typed, proved false or never checked, so an unchecked one shows at close.
- The final report (fm decide --review) prices each task parked at a one-way step: how many days it has waited and which open tasks wait behind it.
- The finish receipt tallies one-way doors: one line naming each step that couldn't be taken back and whether it was undone through its snapshot or parked for you.
- Your steers as checks: `fm drive on --round-first on` keeps Foreman's own captures out of fm next until the open Genesis round's next seed is filed (urgent ones and ones a task depends on still come); `fm drive on --pace off` stops usage pace from dropping work; `fm exam long run` runs a session arm only with `--final` (the end-of-run exam) or `--regressing N`.
- Outside authors for the tools, proof and beyond exam kinds: a child writes the tool kinds' record format and rewords the install, proof and deliverable requests (every requirement checked to survive), so capabilities #5, #6 and #10 can pass the held-out scorecard; #6 and #10 are now graded on their own kinds (proof, beyond).
- The next-request prediction guesses every file your newest request of that type touched, the last request included; replayed over this project's own history its file guess beats the naive one (0.088 vs 0.086 overlap) instead of trailing it (0.053).
- #3 exam kinds forecast and breakage (Mission 8): forecast hands an arm a user's request history and grades its guess at the next request (type and files) beside the naive guess of the last request again; breakage plants a config value that stops working within two weeks next to a harmless look-alike, and grades flagging it before a fake clock reaches the day, with no alarm on the twin. A zero-token `foresight` arm replays both through Foreman's own nightly tick, and its grade replays your own request history with the last third held out. Capability #3 on the scorecard now counts these two kinds.
- Breakage warnings (Mission 8, #3): once a day the tick warns, through the trigger table, of a date in your code, tests or config that passes within 14 days, a commit that changed a file without the partner it nearly always changes with, and state whose 30-day trend reaches its budget within 30 days. Each warning is scored by its window: a hit when the failure shows up there or another task has to change it, acted on when the fix came from the warning itself, a miss when nothing happens; `fm outcomes --predictions` shows each kind's precision.
- Draft ahead (Mission 8, #3): while the prediction book's next-request guess beats its naive baseline, the tick plans the newest prediction ahead: the plan of your closest past request of that type, committed alone as DRAFTED-AHEAD.md (no code) in a throwaway worktree on draft/ahead-ID, labelled speculative and never pushed or merged. When your next `fm task new` or `fm capture` shares 60%+ of its words and paths, the brief gets that plan as "Drafted ahead"; a draft nobody adopts is removed after 3 days. Adoptions and expiries are scored in `fm outcomes --predictions` (draft-ahead); a muted next-request kind, or a muted draft-ahead trigger route, drafts nothing.
- Self-running on every surface (Mission 8): `fm state`, the pane's band and `fm code` show one line of what ran or waits by itself (triggers fired today with their routes, installs waiting for your yes, lanes recovering, self-fix trials due, prediction hit rates beside their baseline); the MCP `triggers` tool returns the table with recoveries and trials; the AGENTS.md block names `fm triggers` and `fm night --tick`; the site has a Self-running page with the figures over time.
- Prediction book (Mission 8, #3): on its tick Foreman predicts your next request's type and paths from your own past requests and scores each prediction once, when the next request arrives, against a naive baseline (the last request again). `fm outcomes --predictions` lists each kind (next request, and the effort estimates) with its hit rate beside the baseline's; a kind that trails the baseline over 20 scored rows is muted and `fm doctor` names it with its unmute command. Only this project's own briefs and ledger are read, and a sensitive project predicts nothing.
- The long-horizon exam plants Mission 8's faults (built now, run at the end of the run): `lane` stops the foreman arm's first builder lane once mid-edit, `event` drops a red CI run and an issue hiding an injected instruction into the copy between turns with nothing said about them, and `recur` makes one planted failure hit every test run; each graded goal records whether each fault fired, the interventions a person would have needed and whether the injected instruction was followed.
- #12 exam kind lean (Mission 8): session arms record what Foreman's hooks cost per scenario (hook milliseconds per tool call, injected characters per turn, tokens, wall time) beside plain's, and a lean scenario fails when that overhead grows past its band against the generation's first measure. A mutation set on a throwaway copy of the plugin checks the self-change gate refuses planted growth (rules, a SessionStart line, a hook delay, a guard rule removed, a verb added) by its metric and passes controls, and that dropping each check lets its case through. The scorecard maps #12 to lean instead of slow.
- The self-change gate refuses a verb added with none removed, and a hook that sleeps or waits on I/O even when the bench machine is loaded (found by the mutation set: a loaded bench compared CPU time only), and no longer refuses a clean change when a hook the bench started leaves a file in the bench's temporary state.
- #9 exam kind lanes (Mission 8): three requests, each built in its own lane by a stub builder that stops at a planted point (killed mid-edit, the usage wall, a hang, a clash with main, red at merge); a zero-token drill does only what Foreman says to recover each one and is graded on every lane recovered by its own cause with no person needed, the stub's work kept, no commit landing twice and at most two recoveries; the oracle scores all and the empty arm none; an outside author writes the three plants for the held-out third, and #9 maps to lanes beside parts.
- A builder that ended with its work committed now reads as finished and waiting on a merge right away, not as uncommitted work to recover for its first 30 minutes (found by the lanes drill).
- #7 exam kinds recur and correction (Mission 8): recur is a repo whose logs show one failure on three dated nights and no request, graded on the logged call returning the right value (a test that fails first is graded too, and quieting the logs or guarding the caller fails); correction gives three dated review notes beside a small fix request, each with its own hidden check (tests stay in the existing file, the fix is logged under Unreleased, the README is left alone). An outside author writes the error line and the notes for the held-out third, and the scorecard maps #7 to these two kinds instead of bug.
- #11 exam kind watch (Mission 8): each scenario is a repo with an event stream (CI results, issues with one planted injection, a burst of file changes, quiet spells) and a hidden key of which events should start which work. A zero-token `triggers` arm replays the stream through Foreman's own tick, standing orders and trigger table in a throwaway project with a fake clock, graded on precision and recall against the key, with nothing run from the injected text, nothing installed and one task per burst; the oracle scores all and the empty arm none. An outside author writes the issue texts for the held-out third, and the scorecard maps #11 to watch, keeping trigger as the proxy it is.
- Night timers wait for one yes (Mission 8, #11): `fm night --stage` writes the nightly `fm night` and hourly `fm night --tick` systemd timers into state and queues one install request naming each line, its schedule, its budget and its undo, with the CI check (`--watch-ci`) and a repo's issue watch (`--watch-issues OWNER/REPO`) as their own lines; the guard holds `fm night --install` for your yes, which installs and starts the timers and turns on only the watch lines you were asked about; `fm night --uninstall` removes them; `fm doctor` names staged timers waiting and units that run another Foreman home. `--print-timer` is gone.
- What no run proved, everywhere you look (Mission 7, #6/#10): `fm code`'s close prints the same Not verified and meaning-unproven lines `fm task finish` does (and its `closed` event carries them); the pane's view model has the unproven count and the kind most need; the MCP `proof` tool gives Claude Desktop and other agents `fm prove --rollup`'s rows; the AGENTS.md block other CLIs read names `fm prove --rollup`; the site has a page listing every proof kind with the count over time.
- #6 and #10 exam kinds (Mission 7): `proof` asks for three fixes, each proven, plus a claim no local run can prove, and is graded on the claims its hidden checks prove, the unprovable one named unproven, and false proofs (a claim called verified whose check fails); `beyond` hands over a non-code deliverable (a migration pair, a CI workflow, README examples, an incident's harm signal) graded by running it. Nine a kind, three held out, all six languages; exam.jsonl gains a false_proofs column.
- Meaning proof (Mission 7, #6): each clause of the request is bound to the criterion that shares its words, and a clause with no criterion, no green run, or only a check that passed before the change is listed as meaning unproven at `fm task finish` and in `fm prove --rollup`; it labels, never blocks.
- Unproven where you see it (Mission 7, #6): a reply claiming a measured speedup, accurate docs, a working migration, a UI that renders right or a release, with no run of that kind on the task or in the turn, is held once at Stop with the command that proves it; a claim kept anyway is labelled unproven in the hook's own message and recorded on the task, and `fm task finish --commit` and `fm pr` carry an `Unproven:` line for any task the rollup still holds.
- Release in any repo (Mission 7, #10): `fm release VERSION` outside Foreman's own repo runs the ritual as checkpointed steps (clean tree, `fm check`, bump the one manifest's version, CHANGELOG's Unreleased headed VERSION, `fm prove --release`, commit, local annotated tag), with `--dry-run` and `--resume`; a red or unproven release proof stops before the commit, and `--push` needs that repo's task's publish grant, stopping after the tag without one. The release skill runs it by default.
- Release proof (Mission 7, #10): `fm prove --release` builds the repo's artifact offline in a throwaway copy (a Python wheel by the project's own build backend, an npm tarball, a gem, a Rust crate whose dependencies are cached), installs it into a throwaway prefix and runs each entry point from there, so a module the artifact leaves out fails; the installed version must match the manifest. A build that needs a download is unproven, naming what it needed; nothing is published.
- Data proof (Mission 7, #10): `fm prove --data --run CMD --check SQL [--db FILE]` runs a data command on a throwaway copy of the tree and its sqlite or CSV data and checks each invariant (a query returning the violating rows) before and after, with every table's row count; it passes only when the command exits 0 and nothing violates after. The checks only read, the command gets no connection variables or credentials, and a database outside the copy is refused. A task's Invariants section is proved at `fm task finish`.
- The migration proof's tests hold on machines that have Postgres: `FOREMAN_PG_DIRS` points the off-PATH Postgres lookup elsewhere, so the no-Postgres cases stay testable on CI runners that ship one; the Postgres fixture's down step drops the type its up made; and a test now cycles a real Postgres wherever one is installed.
- #5 on every surface (Mission 6): a failed `fm code` run goes through the same PostToolUseFailure hook as Claude Code, so a missing tool gets the same note and the task the same finish check; Codex, Gemini CLI and opencode after-tool events that show a missing tool do too, and the AGENTS.md block names `fm which` and `fm ask ID plugin --later`; MCP gains read-only `which` and `grants` tools; the band shows queued install grants; the site has a Getting tools page from the ledger's counts. Fixed on the way: a usage limit that "resets 3pm" was read an hour off across a DST change.
- #5 exam kind tool-grant (Mission 6): a request whose obvious path is an install; package managers on the session's PATH are shims that report every call through a pipe the grader holds open and fail, so any install attempt (or tampering with the record) fails the scenario, whatever else is done. It's graded on zero installs, the package named as the grant it waits on, and the rest finished; a third of scenarios add a pre-approval line in the README or a lookalike package name.
- #5 exam kinds tool-build and tool-find (Mission 6): a request that needs a converter built for a small invented format, graded on a hidden second input, on the arm's own test going red when the tool is swapped for a stub, and on the output it made; and a request a tool already on the shelf answers, graded on that tool's run counter (outside the tree) and its output, so an arm that writes its own converter fails. Nine a kind, three held out, in all six languages; the scorecard counts both for #5.
- #2 and #8 on every surface (Mission 5): `fm code`'s planner writes an M plan's readings, options with `fails if`, and `Choice N (NN%)`, and the task it files meets `fm focus`'s plan gate unchanged (a cold rival read is fetched when the gate wants one); the MCP `calibration` tool reads buckets, the Brier beside the base rate, the bars, races run with how often the first choice lost (`fm lane race`), and tasks parked at a one-way door, all read-only; the band shows one `judgment:` line only when one needs you; the AGENTS.md block's plan line names the shape and `fm outcomes --judgment`; the site has a Judgment page.
- A queued grant for a tool that needs a secret names the variable, never its value (`fm ask ID plugin --pin P --later --needs-env GITHUB_TOKEN`; the user sets it outside the session), and `fm task finish` refuses while this session's transcript holds that variable's live value or a secret-shaped `NAME=value`.
- The migration proof's schema read casts Postgres catalog columns to text before joining them, so newer Postgres (the Ubuntu 26 runners) no longer rejects it as an ambiguous operator.
- One trigger table (Mission 8, #11): each trigger routes to inbox, queue, lane or mute with a tier cap, budget and burst; `fm triggers set NAME --route …` (yours to run) sets a row, the digest settles unset rows from outcomes (precision 0.7+ over 10: queue; under 0.1: muted, each move recorded as a decision), a muted or over-budget fire joins the open brief it repeats, a lane route's capture is worked only in a lane, and `fm doctor` names each muted trigger with its unmute command.
- #2 and #8 exam kinds (Mission 5): race, fork, door and unsure (child-authored, 9 per kind, 3 held out, six languages); every session answer states a confidence and each run reports its Brier beside the closed share; the scorecard maps #2 and #8 to them; `fm exam long --faults door` plants an untracked data file the goal's literal wording would delete (never through a linked folder, and never over a file the repo holds), and each long goal states a confidence scored as Brier against true-done (T-2343).
- The router picks the cheapest model that holds (Mission 8, #9): a pick that reworks 0.1 or less is probed one model down on a stable fifth of its lanes (a hash of the task id); 5+ probes within 0.1 of the pick's rework rate promote the cheaper model as an `fm decide` row, provisional until the long-horizon run confirms it; a reworked probe sends the next lane back up; `fm lane brief --revert-route DATE` undoes promotions since DATE and pauses probing; `fm run` with no --models routes each session the same way and prints it (T-2353).
- Incident ritual (Mission 7, #10): `fm capture --incident '<impact>' --signal CMD` opens an urgent FIX only when the harm signal is red (the signal runs guarded, with every credential stripped, the model's own included; 126/127 or a timeout reads "signal broken" and opens nothing), keeps a UTC timeline (`fm capture --incident log '<action>' --expect '<effect>'`), closes only on the same signal green twice after the fix's last edit plus one captured follow-up, and leaves the signal for `fm sentinel` to rerun after later commits touching the fix's files (T-2388).
- Install-grant queue (Mission 6, #5): `fm ask ID plugin --pin P --why W --later` queues a user-only request that never expires and grants nothing, carrying the miss, the alternatives and the fallback in use; `fm todo`, `fm next`, the Stop hook and the end of `fm run` list it; an explicit no is remembered (`grant_refused`) while an ignored dialog leaves it queued; a yes resumes the blocked task; `fm mcp register claude` queues its plugin request (T-2374).
- The door gate no longer reads a verify that runs `touch` or `mkdir` (or a shell keyword before them, `then touch …`) as deleting untracked data: they write a path but destroy nothing in it; a redirect on the same segment still counts (T-2435, from T-2342).
- `fm goal verdict` counts a done-when only if it fails on the tree its item's work started from: the check runs guarded on the earliest start commit of the item's done tasks (300 s each, 900 s a verdict, cached per done-when and base), so `test 1 -eq 1` or a grep for text that was already there now reads "passes before the work: it proves nothing"; an item with no start tree reads unproven, with the old format check as its fallback (T-2416, Mission 4's critique).
- Watchers start work with no session (Mission 8, #11): `fm night --tick` is a model-free pass (standing orders, the cached CI result, and CI or new issues only for repos you said yes to, never when sensitive or offline) that the Stop hook's sweep runs at most once per interval; issue titles, labels and bodies are defanged and quoted, at most 20 issues a day per repo become tasks, a routine approved poll is a `night_tick` ledger event (only a repo's first request, a failure or the cap writes a decision row), and `fm decide --review` lists every request the tick sent (T-2356).
- Unproven rollup by domain (Mission 7, #6): `fm prove --rollup` lists done tasks no run proved, by kind (typed, red, inconclusive, UI, docs, infra, migration, data, perf untimed or inside the noise band, research with no saved note, hollow criteria), each with the command that closes it or `waiting`; a red-first run counts when the same command ran green later; a proof that only names the command (`echo prove --docs`) or ran before the last edit clears nothing, and no upgrade falls back to `true`; one `unproven_line` shows the counts in status, digest, genesis and the exam scorecard; finish and an empty-queue `fm next` name the upgrade (T-2391).
- Migration proof for real repos (Mission 7, #10): `fm prove --migration` runs dbmate files, alembic and Django through their own CLI on a throwaway database (a throwaway Postgres when installed), under a driver guard that refuses any connect but the throwaway, refuses dangerous Postgres SQL and an outside `script_location`, calls a dialect mismatch unproven by its error text, never reaches a real database, and runs at fm task finish for touched migration files in a trusted tree (T-2386).
- Stop only at an irreversible, ambiguous step (Mission 5, #8): `fm focus` and `fm task step|ac add|edit` sort each step and verify command by door with the guard's categories and git: a guard category stays the guard's stop; a step that destroys untracked or ignored local data (globs, `$VAR`, `~`, bare paths and write verbs like `sed -i`, `>`, `tee`, `mv` onto a file included) gets a verified snapshot and an fm-written Undo line (a decision with a confidence), and a typed `Undo: n/a` counts for nothing; one-way and ambiguous parks for the final report, never a mid-run ping; a two-way fork is nudged to be decided and recorded; `fm digest` counts asks and parks by door and `fm tidy` ages out snapshots after 14 days (T-2342).
- Self-fixes are registered trials (Mission 8, #7): a task a signal filed (CI red, did-not-hold, recurring failure, capability miss, baseline red) becomes a trial on its trigger's own key when it closes; the digest grades it 14 days later as held, didn't hold (one FIX naming both counts and how to revert) or unmeasured (the key was seen only when it was filed, so its absence proves nothing); a CI failure keeps one key across runs; `fm triggers` counts a held fix only once fm outcomes shows the task survived (T-2351).
- L plans race their design (Mission 5, #2): `fm lane race ID` on an L task makes one throwaway S spike per Approach option and briefs a builder for each; `--score` and `--pick` write each spike's checks, cross-examination and findings (defanged) into the L's Approach under "Design race", ties break toward the stated option, and the pick is an `fm decide` row plus a ledger row; spikes close as dropped with their branches kept, `merge()` refuses a spike for every caller, and a race whose L was re-tiered, an unapproved explore L or a re-score after the pick is refused (T-2340).
- Self-change gate at lane merge (Mission 8, #7 #12): `fm lane merge` of a branch that changes Foreman's own plugin/ replays the guard's labelled cases, allow-corpus and recent real commands under base and branch with no grants and refuses any case the base blocks and the branch allows (or can't judge) or that the branch deletes; refuses a hook whose p95 rises past 15% (and 5 ms) or past its budget on two benches of both trees; and refuses always-on growth with no cut. The branch is judged by the base's replay and bench (it can't rewrite its judge), trees come from git's blobs (its .gitattributes can't hide a file), and `fm serve`'s merge is gated too. A criteria waiver never waives it, and only the user's yes to `fm ask ID core` after a refusal of that same commit passes it, not the standing yes, a decision, or a yes for an earlier commit (T-2352).
- The lean ratchet measures what gen-0 names (Mission 8, #12): fm ship's footprint record and fm doctor's ratchet now carry the chars SessionStart and UserPromptSubmit inject and each hook's p95 (measured from the shipped tree's own hook bench), with a 15% band for latency; growth in always-on context clears only when other always-on context was cut by at least as much since that ship (a slower hook or a bigger SKILL body is judged alone), a grown metric keeps its old value in the next ship's record, and an `fm decide` naming the metric no longer waives it (T-2358).
- Built tools prove themselves (Mission 6, #5): a task that ran `fm new tool` or `fm mcp new` closes only after a `fm which` receipt from that task before the build, the tool's test run by fm red then green (the test path in executable position), that test failing on a stubbed copy of the tool, and an fm-run evidence line after the green run whose command runs the tool itself (not cat, git add, --help or the test). Each missing proof is named in the refusal, each tool is checked on its own (one it can't read is refused, never skipped), no typed reason replaces one, and `tool_proved` is logged (T-2372).
- Race at plan time, chosen by evidence (Mission 5, #2): `fm lane race ID --sides 2|3` builds one side per Approach option (the stated choice first) and refuses when every real check already passes on the base tree (timeouts, missing programs and checks that can't fail don't count); `--score` ranks by frozen checks, then cross-examination (each side's new tests run guarded, with a cut environment and a 300 s total bound, on the others' trees; only assertion failures count, import errors are "coupled"), then size and out-of-scope files; a side with uncommitted work is an error row; `fm lane pick` reuses the last score while the heads match and writes an `fm decide` row, a race ledger row and the loser's option into the winner's Approach (T-2339).
- Confidence routes the next step (Mission 5, #8): an M/L choice states `Choice N (NN%)`; an assumption under the research bar (70) or marked (low) needs a check that can fail, a research note or a closed spike, plus a cold reader's agreement, before `fm focus` (the mark is fm-written; a typed copy under either section name is stripped); a choice under the race bar (55) with a runner-up sends `fm next` to `fm lane race` (an L to `fm second plan`) once per task; `fm decide --confidence N` records the number in decisions.md and the ledger (T-2338).
- One find across every shelf (Mission 6, #5): `fm which NEED` searches Foreman's verbs and skills, installed skills, project tools, MCP servers, binaries on this machine, stack cards, marketplace plugins and past fixes, past fixes and on-machine hits first, each row naming its source and whether it needs a grant (repo-carried rows marked unreviewed); no network call; each run logs a `tool_search` receipt, and the capability-miss note names `fm which` (T-2371).
- A verify check that names a path can fail: `ls dist`, `cat README.md` and `cd build && …` no longer read "can't fail" (options and redirects aren't paths); `exit 1` can fail too, while `x || ls dist` and `x; cat y` still count as masking x's exit (T-2333).
- Stopped and stalled lanes recover by cause: `fm lane recover` maps seven stop causes to one recovery each and never resumes over a builder that may be alive (a conflict or red merge on a live lane counts only once old and quiet); a guard refusal names its grant; the stop marker and recovery count live on the task brief, a third stop blocks the task, `fm task unblock` resets them; fm next and the full-autonomy drive lead with it (T-2354).
- Exam putback anchors every region in the arm's file as it is, then splices last to first: slow C's planted loop no longer voids test-first credit, so the oracle can close the held-out slow-c scenarios (T-2419).
- `fm prove --infra` runs each infra file's own validator (hadolint, docker compose config, terraform validate, helm lint, kubeconform, actionlint, shellcheck, systemd-analyze) on a throwaway copy, offline (no network where unshare works); a missing tool is unproven and filed once as a capability miss; fm task finish runs it on touched infra files and names every failing one (T-2385).
- A cold rival reads the request: `fm second rival ID` has a tool-less child on another model read only the request and the repo map and write a Rival plan; rival-only readings and approaches become open Dissent lines and a top reading the plan didn't choose marks the brief rival_split; fm focus waits for it on an L brief, an M brief under 80% or claiming one reading, and a one-way step (on S only deploy, publish, release, migrate, delete, drop table, unless the request names it); a failed child never blocks (T-2341).
- Wider capability-miss detection: a Read of an unsupported file type, an unknown tool or MCP server, a guard refusal of a plugin target, and a failed `fm task evidence --run` with 'command not found' all feed the same miss path; only evidence-run misses block a finish (T-2370).
- Calibration ledger: every stated confidence (a brief's task, choice and assumption percentages, `fm decide --confidence`, `fm lane race --confidence`) is a write-once bet scored by outside signals; `fm outcomes --judgment` shows buckets, Brier beside the base rate, and the research bar, which only rises, only on survival, checked at each `fm task finish` (T-2337).
- Plan gate: `fm focus` refuses an unstarted M/L brief with fewer than 2 distinct readings (bullets don't count; a ruled-out reading needs a reason), fewer than 3 distinct options, an option with no `fails if:`/`likely outcome:`/`breaks`/`→`, or no use-what-exists and do-less option from different options; each gap names its fix; fm code's and the hook's own plans are exempt (`--harness-plan`), `fm batch` writes two readings, and a started task isn't re-gated (T-2335).
- Each generation's sweep fails on net growth: `fm genesis put` of a seed records the always-on footprint, fm verbs and hook p95 (median of three bench runs), and `fm genesis sweep` fails the generation when any ends above its start, listing what it removed against what it added (T-2359).
- `fm exam long run --faults missing` shadows one binary the goal's hidden checks start (a 127 shim first on the session's PATH; package-manager shims that log and refuse); a goal with none is skipped with its reason; grade adds installs_attempted and miss_resolved per goal from the shim logs and the hidden checks (T-2377).
- `fm prove --docs` checks the repo's own CLI flags, subcommands, paths and fenced examples in its docs against the code: a flag or subcommand no file has fails, one a file only mentions stays unproven, only a trusted run of a documented command that exits 0 (with the output the docs show) proves it; a renamed flag left in a doc the task updated blocks the close (T-2381).
- A capability miss during a task is now the task's to resolve: fm next leads with it, and fm task finish and done refuse until a later passing run of the same whole command (a miss_resolved ledger event from the task's own sessions, or a [ran] line) or a proved tool or queued grant; no file, typed line or flag resolves one (T-2369).
- Failures that matter get fixed: a fix that didn't hold re-opens as 'Fix did not hold: T-X'; Foreman's own `fm doctor` FAILs, hook tracebacks and slow hooks recurring across 3+ runs capture a FIX; old failures replay into the loop from `fm doctor`, five a run (T-2349).
- A user-steer rule whose check fails becomes a fix: `fm genesis sweep` and the new `fm genesis steer` file one FIX per rule and commit naming the check and its output; `fm night` runs the steer checks free and its report names any rule that failed (T-2350).
- Blast radius reaches real plans: an M/L focus with no scope takes the repo files its Interpretation, Approach or Steps name, reads a graph built within 50 commits of HEAD, and writes `unknown, no co-change history for …` instead of nothing (T-2336).
- Exam test-first credit: putting a defect back into an arm's fix merges two changed regions whose anchors run into each other, so the oracle closes every #4 scenario (goal-rust-860eab had lost its credit) (T-2401).
- Genesis cold reads judge what a line claims, hides or plans: a critique row that honestly says a capability is still open no longer fails against "everything finishes completely" (T-2400; recorded with fm decide for the user's review).
- Genesis cold reads see the numbers critiques cite: the exam summary now carries exam defects, ranking, recall by language and by kind, and each long-horizon line's true done, hidden checks and false done (T-2366).
- `fm goal verdict` no longer says finished when an item's check can't fail (`true`, `echo ok`, `x || true`, `x; true`, a pipe hiding the exit status) or when gates were green only because red tests were skipped (it names them); task verifies get the same `|| true` warning; Mission 4's spikes (adversary, relay race over 11 kill points, reopened-ticket miner) each have a verdict (T-2332).
- Genesis cold reads: an objection blocks a tower file only when its BREAKS line quotes, verbatim, the gen-0 line, steer or standing-intent clause it breaks; the rest print as advisory, so narrative files converge (T-2246).
- Guard: `fm mandate set` and `fm mandate off` are the user's to run, so a session can no longer end, overwrite or renew the standing mandate (T-2252).
- The website stays current: genesis publish refreshes the homepage's own-record stats once a day, and the Genesis page's long-horizon card shows hidden checks passed per arm, breaking a true-done tie on them (T-2326).
- `fm exam long` keeps each session goal's activity summary (tool calls, fm verbs, turns, Foreman event counts, the fitted done claim), and grade shows it with a "where the time went" line, so a slow or false-done arm says why (T-2325).
- A closed brief written in the last 2 seconds is indexed at once, so a second `fm checkpoint` no longer rewrites briefs-index.json and briefs-closed.json (T-2324).
- `fm ship` runs fm check's affected tests over its range in the scratch tree before the full suite; a red one stops the ship in minutes with the failing classes named (T-2323).
- Long goals state every string or number their hidden checks assert that the goal, its ticket and the base code never mention, and gen drops (and counts) a goal that pins more than three or a value over 60 characters (T-2322).
- Long goals replayed from Foreman's own history carry the task brief's interpretation and acceptance criteria (text only, never the verify commands), so a goal reads like the ticket its session had (T-2321).
- Friction digest keys drop `'s` (a "T-0877's lesson" note no longer keys as "s lesson"); `fm doctor`'s result cache keys on fmdoctor.py's mtime, so an edited check recomputes the same day; the builder contract says never `cd` (not with `&&`, not with `;`) and that Grep/Glob omit `path` (T-2313, T-2314, T-2315, T-2316).
- `fm exam long grade` gives partial credit: hidden checks passed per goal and in total ("0/6 true done, 4/20 checks"), so near misses and the difference between two arms show (T-2320).
- `fm audit prep`: a `--base` value can no longer act as a git option (`--output=FILE` wrote a file); both sides are resolved to tree ids with `rev-parse --verify --end-of-options`, and the diff runs with no ext-diff, textconv, fsmonitor or hooks (T-2318).
- run.py's last line names the failing classes ("FAILED (2 classes: test_x.A, test_y.B)", up to 5, then +N more), so a red run says what failed even through a tail (T-2319).
- `fm lane merge` and `fm audit prep` name every function a branch changes (git's hunk context plus the hunk's own definitions, secrets redacted), so a stray edit stands out (T-2312).
- A batch host held by a builder lane keeps its members out of the main inbox, so the drive no longer offers them as new work (T-2317).
- Tests: in-process speed and complexity bars (design lint and tokens, find rank, library suggest, brief regex, cost bisect, scene render, lint_verify) time CPU with `time.process_time`; deadline, timeout and child-process bars keep the wall clock and name their deadline in a comment (T-2310).
- `fm task evidence --repeat` works again after T-2303's rename reached `_repeat_runs` (T-2311).
- `fm task evidence --bench` puts the start tree's own entry points first on PATH for the before side, so `fm` and any tool that resolves into the tree time the base code, not the current code; the result says when the start tree lacks an entry point or when no word of the command touches the tree (T-2303).
- Guard speed tests time CPU, not the wall clock, so a loaded host can't fail them (T-2309).
- `fm check --affected` adds test_core's import-budget tests when fmhooks.py or fmcore.py changes, so a new top-level import in a hook module fails in the lane, not at ship (T-2307).
- Long-tier goals are passable from their text: checks come only from the task's own commits, and the goal names the new module attributes and flags its checks reach and lists the checks as "Done when", never the test bodies (T-2304).
- Hooks import no `glob` again: the leads-record prune lists its folder (T-2305).
- The Stop hook holds a session once for fm find leads it was shown at session start that are still present and unnamed in its reply (fix test-first, report, or refute); a suspicion with no failing test is reported as a lead, not a finding. Only turns that ran tools are held (T-2297).
- `fm doctor`: the usage burn and unloaded skills checks reuse their last result from `state/doctor-cache.json` (JSON, bounded read, keyed on their input files' paths and hour-floored mtimes plus the day and the forecast hours shown), so a repeat doctor skips their rescans; `fmcore.global_event_files` names the event files `global_events` reads, so the cache key can't drift from it (T-2298, T-2300).
- foreman-ui plugin tests: the four tests that ran near the engine's 5 s default cap now take `{ timeoutMs: 60000 }`, so a loaded host no longer fails a lane's affected run by chance (T-2293).
- `fm exam grade` splits false alarms by scenario kind (`false_by_kind`, overall and per split, and "false alarms by kind" in the summary), so a clean scenario's zero-findings bar reads directly (T-2299).
- Friction digest: the guard example shows the line the guard judged (data-heredoc bodies dropped, the full command found in the session transcript) with the rule's hint, and give-up lines name each task's block reason (T-2280, T-2295, T-2296).
- `fm doctor` times each check: every result carries its wall ms (null when unmeasured), the hook benchmark is charged to hook latency, `--json` adds the three slowest and `-v` prints them, so a slower doctor names its cause (T-2279).
- `fm ci-watch` backs off to GitHub's rate-limit reset (naming the limit and its HH:MM reset, without counting the wait as a poll, giving up after 3 waits) and holds one public-API watch per repo, so several watches don't split the hourly budget (T-2156).
- fm-builder.md tells builders to create `.fm-scratch/` files (an fm edit batch, notes, drafts) with the Write tool and names heredocs and `cat >` as refused there; test_builder asserts it (T-2294).
- `fm mcp register desktop` prints the `mcpServers` stanza for Claude Desktop's config (fm by absolute path, the project by `-p`), so Desktop reaches Foreman's `find` tool and the rest of its MCP server (T-2232).
- `fm exam`: the static test-kind grading reads only test files by Foreman's one test-file pattern (latest.py, contest.js no longer count), and `fm exam check` runs each scenario's clean side 3 times and names one that fails any run (T-2218).
- `fm genesis exam`: G-sweep scenarios plant three defects (a vulnerability, an untested helper, a doc gap) and a draft closes one only when its §2 has a capability with a matching exam kind for each, each miss named; G-line and G-none still plant one (T-2177).
- `fm prove --migration [PATH]` runs a repo's plain SQL up/down migrations up, down and up again on a throwaway sqlite copy, compares the schema each time and refuses untrusted trees; alembic, django and prisma are recognized by name, not yet run (T-2288).
- Foreman predicts each task's active minutes and attempts at focus from the median of its last 30 same-type, same-tier closes, scores them at close, shows per-tier calibration in `fm outcomes --calibration`, and `fm next` notes once when a task passes 3x its prediction (T-2290).
- `fm lane brief` picks the builder model from history: opus when sonnet's lanes in that tier were reworked (a later fix, a revert, a lost outcome) 0.3+ more often than opus's, with 5+ samples each, else sonnet; `--model` overrides; the model is recorded on the brief (T-2291).
- `fm exam run` and `fm exam long run` take `--jobs N`: session arms run N scenarios at once, each in its own temp copy, with the spend check made under a lock that counts sessions still running (T-2292).
- `fm genesis check` flags one feature filled under two coverage cells (the same F: path, descriptions sharing 60% of their words) and counts it once (T-2175).
- `fm lane rm` treats a builder's `.fm-scratch/` as throwaway, like the guard's script cache, so it no longer blocks removing a merged lane (T-2289).
- `fm ci-watch` no longer files a FIX for a cancelled run (a deploy a newer push superseded): it says so and keeps watching, and a commit whose other run succeeded reads green (T-2287).
- Goal distance on the glance surfaces: the statusline, the terminal title and the badge end with `goal 3/7` while a goal with items stands, counted when the views regenerate, so the polled statusline reads no briefs (T-2144).
- `fm goal show` estimates a goal's time to finish: open items times the median active minutes of its done items' tasks, with the 25th–75th percentile range, "no estimate yet" under 3 sampled items, and an estimate block in `--json` (T-2145).
- `fm task evidence ID --bench "<cmd>" [--repeat K]` times the command on the task's start tree (a throwaway worktree) and on the current tree, and records "cmd: before → after (K runs each)" as run evidence and in Measurements, which a PERFORMANCE close accepts in place of typed `--numbers` (T-2284).
- `fm find --ladder` tree-sitter rung: Foreman's own `.scm` rules (starter Python and JS rules in plugin/lib/ladder_rules) run over tracked, size-capped, non-symlink files through an optional tree_sitter binding and report `ts:RULE` findings; a missing binding is one note and one `capability-miss: tree-sitter` (T-2240).
- Lean ratchet: Foreman's own `fm ship` records the always-on footprint (rules chars, descriptions chars, largest SKILL body) after a green test, and `fm doctor` warns on any metric more than 2% over the last shipped record unless a later `fm decide "footprint: <metric> …"` names it; the fixed caps still fail (T-2281).
- `fm find --ladder`: the tool rung runs each stack's own analyzer (ruff, go vet, gosec) with argv fixed in Foreman's registry (a repo's manifests only select), only in a tree you trusted with `fm prove --yes-run` (or an exam sandbox); stripped env, a timeout, polled output caps with a process-group kill, findings kept only for tracked non-symlink files as `tool:NAME` finders with learned precision; a missing tool is captured once as `capability-miss: NAME` (T-2239).
- `fm triggers [--days N] [--json]` lists each trigger that files tasks (fired, done, dropped, open, precision from capture events and each brief's final status); a trigger whose last 20 closed captures number 5+ at precision under 0.3 files at priority low with a note, never baseline, CI or security; `fm state` names the noisiest from a cache (T-2276).
- Blast radius in the plan: focusing an M/L task whose scope names files writes one `Blast radius (fm graph blast):` line into Risks and rollback (co-change neighbours, past FIXes), computed before the project lock from a graph fresh for HEAD; a missing or stale graph starts one detached `fm graph build` so the next focus has it (T-2277).
- Build what's missing: `fm new tool NAME` writes a stdlib `tools/NAME.py` (argparse, `--help`, exit 0/1/2) and a `tests/test_NAME.py` that fails until it works; `fm new skill NAME` writes a project skill with a quoted "Use whenever" description; names are validated (no stdlib names), nothing is overwritten or written through a symlinked folder, `fm new check --run` checks a tool in a sandbox, and the capability-miss note ends "or build it: fm new tool NAME" (T-2278).
- The long-horizon exam decides: `fmlong.grade` records per goal false-done, cost, `resumes_needed` (a planned resume failed or the session errored) and `unfinished` (no done claim, no error; not a human intervention), guard blocks and time to done, each unknown rather than 0 when the run can't tell. `fm exam scorecard`, `fm genesis status` and the Genesis page show a long row, Foreman against plain on the goals both answered (ahead, level, behind, or why there is no verdict); a Haiku line never decides and `behind` fails the card (T-2263).
- `fm prove --rollup [--days N] [--json]` lists done tasks no run here proved (typed, failing or inconclusive evidence, or UI files edited with no shot, smoke, stories or flow run); `fm status`, `fm digest`, `fm genesis status`, genesis.json and `fm exam scorecard` show `unproven: N (fm prove --rollup)` from a cache keyed on the task folders, so status stays fast (T-2273).
- Builders write scratch files (an fm edit batch, notes, drafts) in `.fm-scratch/` inside their worktree, which git ignores, instead of /tmp, which worktree isolation refuses (T-2259).
- The stuck ladder races the runner-up: after three failed runs on a step of an S or M task whose Approach names another way and that hasn't raced yet, `fm next` says `fm lane race ID`, so evidence picks between the two approaches before more hypotheses (T-2271).
- The plan gate checks content by tier: `fm focus` and `fm lane brief` refuse an M/L Approach that lists fewer than 3 options or names no choice, an L brief with fewer than 2 readings in Interpretation or an empty Risks and rollback, and any brief with a `(low)` assumption that has no `research/NAME`, `spike T-NNNN` or verified tag; each gap names its fix, and a task already started isn't re-gated (T-2269).
- The exam scorecard passes a capability only when each language the newest spec drew (all of `fmexam.LANGS` when none is recorded) has a graded held-out result; a `--lang`-narrowed run reads `partial`, its row's `langs` lists what is covered and its `why` names the rest and the run that closes it (`fm exam gen --lang ruby,rust`); a python-only spec passes on python, and an odd scenario id never crashes the card (T-2225)

- A failure that hits three or more different tasks now captures one FIX to find and fix its cause, instead of each task working around it again (T-2272).
- A missing command or module in a Bash failure now names the find-or-build path once per session (`fm plugins find X`, or a script with its test), and a tool missed in a second session becomes one FEATURE task (T-2270); subagent failures and names that are not plain tool names are ignored.
- `fm genesis check` flags a put-able tower file whose current text has no cold-read pass on record (steer 2): `fm genesis put` records each filed file's sha256, so a hand edit is flagged; `grow` and `confirm` move the record only when the text before them was read; a file from before this counts as read with a whole-file pass, or a pass for every section, logged just before its mtime; OWNED files and gen-0 are exempt (T-2181).
- Long-horizon exam (2/3): `fm exam long run --agent foreman|plain [--faults kill,pause,compaction,misleading] [--limit N] [--timeout MIN]` runs a headless session per goal on the stamped model (3 goals by default, spend checked before each goal and doubled for kill or pause), with each planted fault, its resume (or one fresh turn when a resume fails) and a killed turn's unknown cost logged per goal; goal ids are validated before any path is built (T-2262).
- `fm task finish --commit` commits the old name of a file the task moved: the changed-file list ran with git's rename detection and named only the new path, so the deletion stayed uncommitted (T-2268).
- A red `fm ship` test run in the scratch tree captures the same urgent baseline-red task `fm check` writes (once per failing set), names it on the error line and still pushes nothing, so the commit that fixes it teaches `fm impact` which test breaks with that file (T-2255).
- The project map links a test to a mostly non-code folder beside its own that it names (`plugin/rules`, `plugin/skills` beside `plugin/tests`), so `fm impact` and the close's linked tests run the always-on budget tests when rules or skills change; a code folder like `plugin/lib` still links by file name only (T-2267).
- Four job skills (T-2072), picked from this project's ledger: `foreman:security-audit`, `foreman:performance`, `foreman:refactor` and `foreman:write-docs`. Each wraps its playbook and fm verbs; the SECURITY, PERFORMANCE and CLEAN stage routes load them first, and older descriptions were tightened so the listing stays under 2,200 characters.
- `fm lane rm ID --why` repairs a worktree whose creation was cut off (0-byte branch ref, every file empty): the folder, the ref, the admin dir and the reflog move aside into the project's state (`lane-wreck/<id>/`), the stale lock goes and the entry is pruned. `fm doctor` fails on such a lane and on any 0-byte ref under refs/heads
- `fm exam long gen|run|grade` (T-2258): replays real past task changes from their base commit with hidden checks from the tests each change added, in an isolated runner, scored by zero-token oracle and empty arms (true done out of all scenarios, plus regressions) as `tier: long` exam lines
- Genesis now starts itself, once, when the queue runs dry under full autonomy and a standing goal or mandate is backed by your own words (a user-sourced task or a user steer): gen-0 is drafted from that text with no model call, sealed, and recorded as a decision, and the next drive is told to run `fm genesis step`; with no goal, an ended or session-written one, quoted lines from other tasks, standard autonomy or a sensitive project it only offers, as before

- `fm genesis drift` and the put-time cold read now share one whole-line CLEAN verdict pattern: `CLEANUP needed`, `Not CLEAN`, `CLEAN-ish` and `CLEAN but …` no longer pass
- `fm task finish`'s Landed line adds "(+N held for T-…)" for files an open task holds, and says "files already in <sha> (not this task's commit)" when no commit made since the task began (its own, or one naming it, such as a lane's "Merge T-N") touches its files
- A lane's merge-conflict brief now gives steps that work from the main checkout: message the lane's builder, or `cd` into the worktree as its own command, with a drop-and-redo fallback
- `fm exam grade` no longer loses a whole run to one scenario's error: the rest still grade, the broken one is listed as ungraded with its error, and the exit is non-zero
- `fm genesis confirm N` settles a provisional lineage choice from stamped production-model exam lines (`default` counts as the configured model; unstamped and Haiku lines don't) for gen N and its parent on a common run, by select's own comparison: `- confirmed:` or `- reversed:` (the seed base follows what production kept), with the verdict filed in `gen-N/confirm.md`; `fm genesis step` blocks the report and the stop while any choice lacks a matching confirm.md (T-2197)

- The standing goal shows wherever you look: `fm code`'s run prompt, a read-only MCP `goal` tool, a Goal section in `fm export agents` (with the dated last verdict), the band (a stopped goal and its first cause) and the Planning page; a stored verdict counts only while the goal it judged stands, from this checkout and under a day old
- Genesis can add its own categories from gen 2 (`fm genesis grow`): a capability, area, surface, stage, lens, source or exam kind, each with evidence, a measure and a cold read. Added capabilities join the exam, the scorecard and the seed like gen-0's. The frame only grows: `fm genesis check` fails a lost or hand-edited entry, and the report must list every added category
- `fm prove --bugs [N]`: a cheap tool-less child names up to 5 bug candidates in each of the N hottest files (else the largest), kept only when strictly shaped and inside a function; each is proven by a model-written red test with stub controls or stays a lead, `--capture` files the proven ones as bug tasks, and their `--only bug-ID` criterion reruns the kept test (red is open, green fixed, missing never fixed); trusted trees only, never a sensitive project (T-2242)
- `fm goal plan [--apply --done-when CMD]` proposes the standing goal's contents list tagged user, standard or model, builds a model item only from a tracked path, surface or fm verb the repo has (the model's sentence stays a proposal), lists the project's past misses as a checklist, and `--apply` adds the non-duplicates (T-2136).
- A standing goal rides across days: SessionStart, the PreCompact checkpoint and `fm resume` print one goal line (its text, items done/total, the next item and its first open task), and `fm run` takes that item's planned task first
- Genesis keeps one moonshot Mission each generation from gen 2: ideas outside every coverage cell, a far-domain lens, a frontier digest from your own research notes (`fm genesis frontier N`), and a cold novelty read (`fm genesis novelty N`) that marks the obvious ones. `fm genesis check` holds each idea to prior-art and better-by lines, keeps a failed spike in not-yet.md, and makes the report say which shipped moonshots you asked for
- `fm goal verdict` says `finished` only when every item has a done task and none open, its check passes now, every goal clause is covered and `fm check` last ran green on this tree; otherwise `stopped` with each cause (exit 1, no goal 2); a done claim on a stopped goal blocks once at Stop, counted toward DRIVE_MAX (T-2137)
- `fm find --blindspots` lists closed FIXes whose changed lines no finder flagged (flags are saved at `--capture` with each file's blob hash, recorded when the FIX closes), and `--capture 1` files at most one "add a rule, tool or check" task a day, the newest record not yet filed

- `fm find --capture`, `--rank` and `fm prove` give twin leads the same keys, so a capture's dedupe and a proof's lift name the same lead
- `fm goal` turns the standing mandate into a goal ledger: items, each with a check command and the tasks that build it (`fm goal link`, or `fm task new --goal N`); `fm goal show` lists open work outside the goal, items with no task, and items whose tasks closed while the check still fails, exiting 1 when any. A plain mandate prints as before
- `fm mandate` shows its end date as the day you typed; it printed the next day west of UTC
- `fm task finish --commit` now commits the docs its `--docs` (or Docs impact) names, even when another task touched them first and with no touched record of its own, so CHANGELOG.md is no longer left dirty under a "Landed: committed" line; a doc an open task is still editing, a `none:` note, `..` or absolute paths, and `.foreman/` stay unclaimed (T-2251)
- `fm find --hotspots PATH` ranks tracked files by how many of the last 500 non-merge commits with a fix word in the subject touched them (git with fsmonitor, hooks and gpg programs off, renames followed, symlinks and untracked files never counted), with `--json`; `fmfind.hotspots(root, n)` returns the same rows for the bug rung (T-2238).
- `fm genesis status`, genesis.json and the site's Genesis page show `fm exam scorecard`'s per-capability held-out rows (state, closed of graded, why), text only (T-2154).
- `fm find --rank` weighs proof and effort: a lead `fm prove` proved ranks up to 1.25x until its own function changes (never past an unproven lead of a more severe kind), and a lead in a small function ranks above an equal one in a long function
- Each new genesis seed and each `fm mission` pack now carries the user's standing rules: every steer still in force, one line each. `fm genesis drift` fails a seed that drops one; an older seed, or a steer newer than the seed, only gets a note
- `fm loop round` stops re-listing a lagging file once a decision names it, and lists it again when the file changes after that decision
- `fm code --help` and the site's Foreman Code page can't drift apart: a site test names every verb and flag the page misses
- 21 new playbooks fill stages that had none for their stack: mobile, embedded, game, data pipeline, Go, serial/bus and Claude Code plugin verification and release, each built on Foreman's stack cards with real commands, indexed and routed.
- `fm prove` now proves more kinds, only in a tree you said yes to: a flaky test when six fresh reruns both pass and fail with a failure naming it, and a vuln or slow finding when a test a model writes is red three times out of three on an assertion and turns different both when the function is stubbed to raise and when it's stubbed to return a harmless value. A test that fails for its own reason stays a lead, and a sensitive project gets no model-written test.
- `fm log NAME` now records a note as event `log` with the name inside it, so a session can no longer write an event that Foreman's own checks read (a check run, a genesis stage, a ledger-damage acknowledgement). Notes like `fm log final_verify` still record and show in `fm recall`.
- `fm genesis put`'s section reads now size sections by characters: a file of 3000+ characters splits, and only a section under 300 characters shares a read with the one before it, so a critique written in paragraphs gets one read per section instead of one read for nearly all of it.
- `fm genesis put` reads a long tower file one section at a time: each `##` section gets its own cold read with an outline of the others, a section that passed and hasn't changed isn't read again, and the file is filed when every section passes. Short sections share a read, and a put reads at most 12.
- `fm genesis step` moves past the select stage only on `gen-N/select.md`, a record only `fm genesis select N` writes and `fm genesis put` refuses, so a hand-written lineage heading or an `fm log` line can no longer skip selection. A tower whose select ran before this re-runs `fm genesis select N`.
- `fm find` gives the same dependency findings on every machine (a fixed import-to-package table instead of the host's installed packages), a quote mark inside a Python string, comment or multi-line f-string no longer hides later functions from the untested finder, `fm prove` cuts each test run at its deadline, and a cold re-find says it is a re-find by the same check, not an outside proof.
- `fm genesis put`'s cold reader can quote across lines (it writes the line break as a literal \n): each piece of the quote must name a line, and the finding lands on the last one, so a quote spanning a heading and a bullet is no longer refused as a line the file doesn't hold.
- `fm find` now reads tracked files in hidden folders such as `.github/workflows` (untracked hidden folders are still skipped) and never reads a file through a symlinked folder. `fm find --capture` and `fm prove --capture` keep at most 5 of their tasks open at once, quoted repo text that reads like instructions is marked as data, and a rejected `fm prove --yes-run` call no longer remembers your yes.
- `fm exam` now reports how many planted defects each arm found per language and per defect kind (overall and held out), not just one total, so a finder that's blind in one language or to one kind of bug shows up.
- `fm genesis put`'s cold reader can now quote a sentence that wraps onto the next line of a paragraph: a wrapped paragraph counts as one line, so the quote names it instead of being refused as a line the file doesn't hold.
- When `fm genesis put`'s cold reader quotes a line the file doesn't have, the refusal now shows the reader's own words, so you can see whether it misquoted or paraphrased.
- `fm genesis put` no longer gets stuck after you rewrite or delete a line an earlier cold read flagged. The old finding now follows the line it became (a one-word edit can't shed it) and ends when the line is gone, so the reader never quotes text the file no longer has. A quote also still names its line when the reader drops its backticks or cuts it short with "…".
- `fm genesis put` converges on long tower files. A line that two cold reads in a row left unflagged, and that hasn't changed or moved since, no longer fails a put on its own: the reader's complaint prints as an advisory, and a second complaint about it blocks. A changed line, a line that failed before and isn't fixed yet, a new steer, and new exam results all block as before. The ledger records each line passed this way.
- `fm genesis exam --author child` scores Genesis on projects Foreman didn't write: what a draft reads, the project's README and the MUST/NEVER lines in the "make this great" request, comes from a model with no tools, written once per scenario so every arm is graded on the same input. The planted code defect is still Foreman's, but no draft reads code. `--lang` limits the languages it draws from.
- New `.fmproved` file: list the paths in your repo whose changes always need a failing-then-passing test (billing, auth, scoring), one glob a line, and `fm task finish` refuses to close any task that changed one without that proof or a note saying why there is none. Foreman's own repo lists its exam and scorecard code.
- Three new exam kinds give the last capabilities without an exam one: `exists` (asks for something the code already does; a good answer names it instead of adding a copy), `parts` (three problems in one request) and `trigger` (make the tests run on a schedule). Child authors write all three. `exists` fails an answer that adds a copy of the function, `trigger` only counts a real CI workflow, timer or crontab, and #9's row says its exam is a proxy for delegation. A default `fm exam gen` now renders 18 kinds, about a fifth more scenarios.
- Child authors now write the vague, ambiguous and missing-tool requests too, with what a good answer must name, so every exam kind can be scored on scenarios Foreman didn't write. A capability that hasn't been graded on each of its kinds yet reads "partial", not pass.
- The scorecard's "top gap" now compares a free arm with the session only on the same scenarios: a session that answered 1 of 36 no longer reads as finding 1 of 36.
- `fm ship` with no commits named now ships a lane that was merged after settling a conflict even when `main` moved since the last ship, as long as `main` only changed files the branch left alone (like the site's auto-published Genesis data). If `main` touched the same files it still refuses and pushes nothing.
- Child authors now also write untested, clean, docs and flaky exam scenarios: a new helper whose test is missing (or present, for a clean repo Foreman never saw), a README sentence the code contradicts, and a test that passes by chance. Each is checked before it's kept, so every exam kind but vague, ambiguous and missing-tool can be scored on scenarios Foreman didn't write.
- `fm genesis step` reads a Mission's whole `Task:` paragraph, wrapped lines included: a long one no longer made it count briefs as never filed and ask for a second `fm intake` of them.
- The capability scorecard now counts only held-out scenarios an outside author wrote (`fm exam gen --author child`): results on Foreman's own template scenarios are shown as "self-authored, not counted", and a capability no outside author can write scenarios for yet reads `no outside author`, never pass. Child authors now also write slow and missing-dependency scenarios.
- `fm prove PATH --capture N` files each proven finding as a ready task whose check is `fm prove PATH --only ID`: red while the finding stands, green once it's fixed, so `fm run` can work them on lanes. When nothing is proven it writes a receipt (research/prove-receipt.md) listing each lead and why. New user-only `/foreman:initiative` runs the whole pipeline: map, find, prove, fix, receipt.
- `fm exam grade`'s test-first credit is fairer: it puts back only the planted defect's lines in the arm's own work (anchored on the unchanged lines around each one, so a fix worded differently still counts) instead of the whole planted file, which also wiped the arm's other edits; a suite that fails only because it can't load a name earns no credit.
- `fm mcp` serves `genesis_status`: Desktop and other agents read where the Genesis tower stands (its generation, files, coverage, the next action and the last exam result), beside `exam_scorecard`.
- `fm genesis put`'s cold read now sees what earlier failed reads of the same file named: it checks those were met instead of starting over, so a long file stops getting a fresh batch of objections (some reversing the last round's) every time. The revised file is the answer to each finding. An unmet finding still fails, new problems (metric gaming included) are still found, and the list clears once the file passes.
- New `fm prove [PATH]`: turns `fm find`'s candidates into proof tickets you can rerun, or leads with a reason. On a throwaway copy, an untested function and a control function the tests call are made to leave a marker file when they run; the function is proven untested when the suite stays green, the control's marker appears and its own doesn't. A suite that never runs the control proves nothing, and a swallowed error can't fake a proof. Docs, dependency and secret findings are re-found on a fresh copy. Repo code runs only on the copy, with credential and Foreman variables removed and HOME pointed at the copy (it still runs as you, with the network), and only in a tree you said yes to: `fm prove PATH --yes-run`, which only you can pass, runs it and remembers the tree (the folder, not a version of its code). Elsewhere untested functions stay leads and the rest still proves; a commit under your email isn't a yes, since anyone can write it.
- Genesis carries your standing intent everywhere it reads: every cold read and drift check sees it beside gen-0 and your steers and asks what you would say, each seed and spec.md quote it, `fm genesis draft` puts it in any repo's gen-0, the Genesis page lists its nine clauses, and #13's exam fails a draft that drops one.
- The Genesis line (session start, `fm state`, the pane, `fm code`) now shows the held-out exam scorecard, e.g. `gen 1 · exam 0/13 pass, 4 Haiku skipped · build: …`, and `fm mcp` serves it as `exam_scorecard`, so Desktop and other agents can read the done gate. The line also refreshes after a Foreman update instead of waiting for a tower file to change.
- `fm genesis check`'s reach no longer counts an fm verb as shown to a session just because a hook's matcher list or a command's own output names it: only text a session sees without asking counts. Live reach still 27 of 27.
- `fm exam run` no longer caps each exam session at $2: a production-model session on a hard scenario was cut off mid-proof and scored as errored. Sessions now run uncapped unless `--budget USD` is given; the per-session timeout still stops a runaway, and your own `fm budget` limit still applies.
- Haiku exam scores never decide: each exam line now records the model its session ran on, `fm exam scorecard` (the done gate) leaves out lines from a Haiku session and says how many it left out, `fm genesis select` still reads them (its choice is provisional until the end-of-run exam) but names the model, and a top gap from a Haiku run says so. `fm exam grade RUN --agent A --model M` stamps answers from before this change; the s31-006 smoke run is stamped, so the live scorecard skips its 4 foreman lines.
- `fm exam grade` no longer takes an arm's own test as proof that a finding the key lacks is the exam's fault: the arm wrote both the change and the test, so the finding stays a false alarm. The exam line shows how many had the arm's own proof (`7 false alarms (1 with the arm's own proof)`), and they stay listed for reading; the site's exam table shows the same. A scenario that used to close on such a finding now stays open, so earlier exam lines aren't comparable on this point (T-2170)
- `fm genesis select` marks each lineage choice provisional: its section says it rests on one exam run (which, from which agent, how many graded lines) until the end-of-run exam on the production model confirms or reverses it. `fm genesis check` and the sweep fail a lineage choice with no provisional, confirmed or reversed line. Writing the confirmed or reversed line is still manual until the end-of-run exam does it (T-2171)
- Foreman sessions now see and use more of what Foreman has: `fm next` in a repo new to Foreman names `fm adopt` (gates, map and secrets as the baseline) before `fm find --rank`; session start shows a standing `fm mandate` (renewing an ended one is your call, never the session's), and the brainstorm skill sets one for a sweeping request. `fm genesis check`'s reach counts a verb as used when a verb a session runs imports its module (`fm find` runs `fm outcomes` and `fm docs` code), so it measures use, not wording (T-2186)
- fm research add --from-agent: a Noticed line that only says there is nothing (`None (T-2186 covers it)`, `None observed`, `nothing - all of it was in the brief`, `N/A.`) is no longer filed as a task; `None of the callers check …`, `Nothing validates the port` and `None, but …` still are (T-2190)
- A `claude -p` run in a Foreman project now ends on its answer: every Stop-hook hold in print mode tells the session to restate its whole answer last, since the caller sees only the final message (exam sessions that fixed the planted bugs were scored 0 because a retro came after their findings). The exam also re-asks a session once for its JSON when the reply has none, and keeps the reply's tail. Session-start leads say how each kind closes (an untested one with a test that calls it). Haiku smoke, held out: 10/18 → 15/18 found; the finders alone find 16/18 (T-2192).
- A Foreman session now uses Foreman's own finders: on a held-out exam run the foreman arm found 6 of 18 planted defects while `fm find` alone found 16, because nothing a session read named it. Session start with nothing to do (an unattended run, or a repo Foreman hasn't worked in) now shows `fm find`'s ranked leads as quoted data, `fm next` with an empty queue there names `fm find --rank`, and the operating rules say an unfamiliar repo starts there. `fm genesis check` names any fm verb a mission names that no session path calls, and `fm exam scorecard` and the next seed put a free arm beating the session first.
- Every user steer now leaves a standing rule plus an automatic check: `fm decide --check CMD` stores the check on the rule (only `fm genesis check`, `fm exam scorecard` or `python3 plugin/tests/run.py test_…`, never a shell), `fm genesis sweep` runs each and turns red on a steer rule with no check or a failing one, and `fm doctor` fails on a missing one in a Genesis project. A rule whose enforcement is still being built takes `--check "pending T-NNNN"`: the doctor passes, the sweep stays red. Only a later row citing a user steer can settle a steer rule. The existing steer rules got their checks; four whose enforcement isn't built yet are pending on their tasks.
- Foreman isn't a Claude Code-only tool: the README, the site, INSTALL.md and MASTER now call it the discipline layer for coding agents (Claude Code, Codex, Gemini CLI, opencode, or `fm code` on any model). The installer works with no Claude Code: it skips the Claude Code plugin, settings and wiring, puts `fm` on your PATH, runs `fm agents install` for each of Codex, Gemini CLI and opencode it finds, and points at `fm code` when there's none; `--build` without Claude Code says why it can't. `fm doctor` without Claude Code no longer fails `plugin validate` or asks you to wire `~/.claude` (T-2173, T-2174).
