- `fm genesis put` files a tower doc only after a cold Haiku read passes it: the reader sees gen-0's goal, MUSTs, NEVERs and DONE-WHEN, your later steers (both `user steer` and `the user's steer` tags), the newest exam score per run and agent, and the redacted file, and checks it for drift and metric-gaming (filler, templated text, format-only checks, progress the exam doesn't show; a file that honestly says what is still open isn't failed for it). A `FAIL:` naming one of the file's lines sends it back with that line and why, and nothing is written; a reply that names lines the file lacks (its why is still shown, so you can fix the point it meant), gives no verdict, or a read that didn't run refuses too. `spec`, `select` and `seed` go through the same read; the stage gates' own records (exam.jsonl, drift.md, sweep.md) come from fm's graders and aren't read again. A refused file isn't held as pending: nothing is written and the older version stays (T-2166).
- `fm genesis check` refuses templated coverage cells: a cell that reads the same as another slot's in its surface column (the area or stage swapped in) or another surface's in its own slot (the surface swapped in) isn't counted filled, and the problem names its twin. Both twins are flagged, except that two cells naming the same shipped file and feature count once. A cell under 3 words besides the area's name is too short. Empty cells list after the bad and templated ones, and the check line shows the templated count (T-2159).
- `fm genesis put` refuses the stage gates' own records: `exam.jsonl` (appended only by `fm exam grade` and `fm genesis exam`) and `gen-N/drift.md` and `sweep.md` (written only by `fm genesis drift N` and `sweep N`), so a hand-written line can't fake a held-out pass and a forged `Verdict: clean` can't pass a stage (T-2155).
- `fm exam scorecard` grades each gen-0 capability on its held-out exam: per §2 capability, the newest result of each held-out scenario of its kinds from graded runs (`--agent`, default foreman), the genesis capability from `fm genesis exam`'s drafts, marked pass, under, too few (under 3 graded) or no exam kind, and exits 1 unless all pass (`--bar`, default 0.8). `fm exam grade` and `fm genesis exam` now record which scenarios they graded, so a held-out miss counts (T-2097).
- A task's close now runs the tests that read a whole folder when it changes a file there: `fm map` links a test to every file under a top-level folder it names as a string (`"site"`), so a `site/genesis/genesis.css` edit runs `test_design_lint` at close instead of first failing at `fm ship` (T-2086).
- `fm tidy` finds projects whose folder is gone: one that lived under a temp dir (a throwaway trial) has its state moved to `state/archive/projects/` on `--apply`; one elsewhere is only reported, since its disk may just be unplugged (T-2091).
- The site header fits at 320 to 419 px without the Barlow font: the brand no longer shrinks under its own text ("Foreman" ran into "Install" at 360 px with the wider fallback face), gaps tighten under 420 px, and under 360 px the header's GitHub link gives way to the footer's; the header test now measures the text, not just its box, on the landing and genesis pages (T-2089).
- `fm genesis export DIR` removes a doc it wrote last time that the tower no longer has; only files its previous `genesis.json` listed, in the tower's shape and not behind a symlink, so the rest of DIR stays yours (T-2084).
- When the queue drains and scout files a find that waits on your yes, the Stop hook tells the session to ask once (`fm ask ID confirm`) and leave it unstarted, instead of the "plan it and work it" meant for ordinary finds (T-2111).
- Genesis seed rows read when the file name is in backticks or the closing pipe is missing (one used to swallow the next row), and a drawer link on the Genesis page that switches generation keeps keyboard focus in the drawer (T-2083).
- `fm exam grade` counts a corrupt or non-object answer file as an errored session instead of an empty answer, which quietly closed scenarios where the right move is to find nothing (a JSON list crashed it) (T-2103).
- A stopped Genesis tower now really stops: `fm genesis seed` refuses to write the next seed until you write one by hand or file the report, instead of only warning (T-2110).
- A Genesis tower now shows on every surface: `fm state` and a new session's note name its next action, the statusline shows `⌂ gen N · stage` (amber when it can't go on), fm code and the pane get a genesis row, `fm ui` raises a signal when it's blocked, and AGENTS.md tells other agents to drive it with `fm genesis step`. The step is cached until a tower file or a task's status changes (T-2118).
- The Stop check for parts of a request with no task now works without `fm clauses` being run: a prompt of 3 to 12 parts has them saved as it is sent (redacted; sensitive projects, slash commands and headless workers skipped), and each stop re-matches them, so a task captured later covers its part; only the session that sent the request is held, each part holds the stop once, and parts too short to match any task ("Please fix it.") are skipped (T-2146).
- `fm check --affected` and a close's affected run now include the tests a file once turned red: when a red full gate's baseline fix closes, the files its fix commit changed are linked to the tests that failed (a fix's CHANGELOG line is a record, not a cause). An edit to MASTER.md now runs test_docs; before, MASTER.md co-changed with every task, so no test was linked and a broken docs count reached the full gates (T-2148).
- `fm genesis step` names `fm genesis spec` after gen-0 when spec.md is missing and a §2 capability maps to an exam kind, so the held-out exam exists before the seed (T-2100).
- `fm genesis draft` refuses a goal given both as a line and in `--file` (it used to drop the line silently), and `--per-kind` is refused on any action but `spec` and `exam`, where it does something (T-2101).
- Genesis now maps a capability to the right exam kind for working unasked: `none` (problems planted, no request: find them on your own), `clean` (nothing planted: change nothing; Calibrated judgment gets it) and `rank` (several planted: fix the worst first). Before, it gave a clean repo the `none` kind, which now plants problems (T-2131).
- A new session's first note is shorter: the usage burn, the blocked pile, an overdue tidy and a guard rule hit again and again now share one `Watch:` line, each pointing at the command with the detail (`fm digest`, `fm tidy`, `fm risk`), and the queue names 3 tasks. On this repo it went from about 1,900 characters to about 1,450 (T-2117).
- The note on a file's 3rd edit in a row (use `fm edit` for the rest) is said in full once a session; later files, and the same session after a compaction, get a one-line form (T-2108).
- `fm friction`'s line for the errors the hooks swallowed now names where the latest one was raised (its innermost file and line, such as `fmhooks.py:1474`), so the digest points at the code, not just the log (T-2107).
- `fm exam` gains two kinds for finishing a goal: `goal` (only a vague goal is given; graded on what a finished version holds) and `resume` (the same goal half done, with PLAN.md on disk and only "continue" given). Saying done with an item unmet is a false done, and breaking an item that was already met counts as broken. With `--author child`, a tool-less child writes the whole answer key: 4 to 6 items, each a code gap with the test that catches it, a missing test, or a README line with a pattern for it (no template item is scored there). A key with too few items, two items on one function, or a pattern that's bad or already matches is dropped with its reason (T-2149). Genesis gives both kinds to Goal to finish.
- When you've run `fm clauses` on a request, a reply that says it's done while parts of it still have no task is held once at Stop, naming them, so a sweeping request isn't called finished with pieces silently dropped.
- `find` MCP tool: other agents and Claude Desktop get fm find's ranked leads for the project or a folder in it; the band shows when your drops have muted a finder; the site has an Initiative page.
- `fm scout --slow` (and `fm night`) now runs `fm find` on your own projects: it files at most 3 leads a day and 3 open, only kinds that prove themselves (an undeclared import, a docs slip, a secret), never one you dropped, and skips trees whose recent commits aren't yours (where others commit too, each lead waits for your yes). Doctor failures seen in a project go to Foreman's own inbox, not yours.
- `fm find --rank` lists the worst first: severity × reach (how many other files name the symbol) × that finder's precision, so a SQL injection always sorts above a missing test or a docs slip. `fm find --capture N` files up to N new leads as tasks tagged with their finder (two leaks in one function are two tasks; one you dropped is never filed again, one you fixed that comes back is), and a finder learns from them: a dropped task counts as a false alarm, a done one that held as a real find, and old outcomes fade over about six months. A finder whose precision falls under 0.3 is muted and its leads are listed last, unfiled, and don't fail the exit code. Usage slips exit 2, never 1 (1 means findings). Leads from a tree outside the project wait for your yes. `fm exam run --agent finders` now lists in ranked order, so `rank` scenarios close on order.
- `fm exam` gains three kinds for initiative with no request at all: `none` (a child-planted bug beside an untested function and a docs slip), `clean` (nothing to find; any finding is a false alarm) and `rank` (a vuln, a missing test and a docs slip; it closes only when the vuln is named first). `--author child` now writes the `none` and `rank` plants too.
- New `fm find [PATH]`: finds untested public code, imports no manifest declares, SQL built from a parameter, membership tests in loops, unseeded randomness in tests, docs naming functions that don't exist, and leaked secrets, in Python, JS, TS, Ruby, Rust and C, with no model calls. Each finding is a candidate until a failing test proves it. It finds nothing on every clean exam app, and `fm exam run --agent finders` grades it.

- `fm exam` no longer counts true findings against an arm: every language's clean app now keeps the edge contract its README states (empty pages below 1, clamp with lo > hi, wide labels, negative values in dedupe, a C label that takes a buffer size), the C and Rust READMEs show a run command that works, and a finding that comes with a test failing on the clean app and passing on the arm's fix is counted as an exam defect instead of a false alarm.
- `fm genesis step` no longer reads a seed whose Missions aren't in the table form (a bullet list, a header with no rows) as a dry generation and skips straight to selection: a seed with no rows must say "no Missions this generation", or step stops and names the table's shape.
- `fm friction` names hook notes averaging over 1,500 characters per injection, including the every-turn SessionStart and UserPromptSubmit ones the repeat line skips.
- `fm genesis drift`'s cold read also sees the standing rules you gave after gen-0 (decisions cited `user steer`), so a Seed that follows them isn't flagged; a steer can only add to gen-0. A drifted line the reader quotes across a wrapped bullet now counts instead of voiding the read.
- Genesis seals your prompt and watches its own privileges: `fm genesis init` records gen-0's hash beside it, so any later edit (even one that blesses a seed's new MUST) or a lost seal fails `fm genesis drift`; `fm genesis sweep` lists the grant kinds the generation used (fm ask, --allow, standing yes) and turns red on a kind no earlier generation used that you didn't grant in this one. The Genesis page shows the seal.
- `fm genesis exam` grades Genesis itself on generated projects: given "make this great" with a couple of planted MUST and NEVER lines, nothing at all, or "super improve this", does the draft cover every MUST, NEVER and section of your Genesis prompt, give the project's planted defect a capability, say what it assumed and leave no capability without an exam kind? It runs the held-out third by default; `--arm plain` runs a plain model told only the format, beside it, with the cost of each. Each arm is scored twice: strictly, and on only what it was handed (a plain model never sees your gen-0); a draft that fell back or errored counts as an error, never as closed, and each scenario prints a progress line. A draft from nothing now says what it assumed. `fm exam run --agent genesis` runs a whole Genesis generation in a throwaway repo, and `--request` gives any session arm your own words.
- Genesis offers itself and knows when to stop: with 10 or more tasks done and the queue drained, `fm scout` offers to start Genesis once (it waits on your own yes, and a no is never asked again); three reverted generations in a row stop the tower and `fm next` says so instead of driving it; `/foreman:genesis` drives a tower from gen-0 to the report; the site's Genesis page shows each generation's drift and sweep verdict (T-2105).
- `fm genesis drift N` checks a generation's seed and Mission files against your Genesis prompt (gen-0): a MUST or NEVER line that isn't one of yours word for word, or a file that doesn't point back to gen-0, fails it; when those pass, one cold read of the seed names any line that weakens or drops your goal, MUSTs, NEVERs or DONE-WHEN. `fm genesis sweep N` runs the end-of-generation check list in one command (genesis check, tasks, lanes, docs, doctor, the gates' pass on this tree, an exam line) into gen-N/sweep.md. `fm genesis step` waits on both until they say clean (T-2104).
- `fm genesis select N` keeps a generation's rewrite only if it closed more exam scenarios than its parent did on the same run, counting the train split only; a tie, fewer, or any scenario the parent closed and the child reopens brings the parent's text back, and a one-scenario margin on a single graded run asks for a rerun first. Each verdict lands in lineage.md and the decision log. `fm genesis seed N` then writes the next generation's Seed itself: Missions ranked by open coverage cells (at most 8, the rest named), every empty cell and not-yet idea, what the critiques asked to change, exam scores and lineage, on top of the newest kept ancestor's seed. `fm exam grade` lines now carry the closed scenario ids and the generation that answered (stamped at run, so a later regrade files under the same one) (T-2102).
- `fm genesis draft` writes a whole Genesis prompt (gen-0) from one line or from nothing: the project's languages, docs, README and your recent corrections, a capability spec written for this project with an exam kind on each line, your MUST/NEVER/DONE-WHEN lines kept word for word and the ones from open tasks carried over (those not written by you marked unconfirmed). It pipes straight into `fm genesis init --file -`. `fm genesis spec` then turns the capability spec into a generated exam with a third held out and files `spec.md` in the tower.
- `fm genesis step` names the Genesis tower's one next action and its command from the tower's files alone: write the seed, write a Mission, intake its briefs, finish the open one, critique it, select into lineage.md, the drift check, the §7 sweep, then the next seed (with `fm evolve --runs 3` every third generation) or the final report after two dry generations. It changes nothing, so after a kill at any point it names the same action; more than 8 Missions, a Missions table that doesn't parse, or a task id that doesn't exist stops it, and briefs already intaken but missing from the Mission's `Task:` line are named instead of a second intake. `fm genesis`, `fm next` while a tower is open (ahead of the forever loop; an unreadable tower falls through), and the site's Genesis page show it (T-2095).
- `fm genesis check` enforces the Genesis grid and ledger: every coverage row has all 64 stage × surface cells, each an existing feature path, an idea with the file it builds on, or N/A with a reason, and every gen-0 capability has its row; every not-yet idea names its blocker, two different approaches tried, what would unblock it and a retry this generation. `fm genesis` shows filled, empty and open counts; the site shows filled cells and waiting ideas (T-2093).
- `fm exam`: a seeded exam of throwaway repos in six languages (Python, JS, TS, Ruby, Rust, C), each with a planted defect (bug, vuln, missing test, slow path, docs drift, flaky test, bad dependency) or a request shape (vague, ambiguous, missing tool, nothing to do), the answer key and hidden checks kept outside the repo and a third of every kind held out. `fm exam check RUN` proves each planted defect is caught and the clean version passes; `fm exam run RUN --agent oracle|empty|finders` grades an agent's findings and fixes against the keys, writes the score to the Genesis exam.jsonl and the site's exam table, and `fm genesis` shows the last score (T-2079). `fm exam gen --author child` has a model with no tools write the bug and security plants itself; one that fails `fm exam check` is dropped and listed. `--agent foreman` and `--agent plain` run Claude with and without Foreman on the same scenarios (`--split holdout --limit N --budget USD`), each score with its cost (counted in `fm cost` and capped by `fm budget`); a session that errors is counted as errored, not silently scored zero.
- `fm genesis live on` keeps the site's Genesis page current: after one publish grant, every tower change and each turn end starts a background publish (at most every 5 minutes; a change inside that window goes out when it ends) that exports the tower into a scratch copy of main and pushes the data (site/genesis only) when something changed; `fm genesis publish` runs one now, `fm genesis` shows the last push, `fm genesis live off` stops it. The test workflow skips data-only pushes (T-2090).
- The Genesis page is part of the site: the same header, nav, footer, stylesheet, type and light/dark palette as every other page, with the tower's own shades mixed from the site's tokens (T-2087).
- Genesis on the site: `fm genesis export DIR` writes the tower's docs (home paths shown as ~, secrets redacted) and a genesis.json of its capabilities, rated gaps, generations, missions, their tasks' progress, exam results and coverage; site/genesis draws it live as the prompt tower (hover a mission to see what it waits on), capability cards, the ambition scale, the exam table, the run's rules and every doc, each box opening a drawer with its full text, in light and dark and at phone width, loading nothing from another host; linked from the landing page (T-2080).
- `fm genesis put` also files not-yet.md and coverage.md, and `--file -` reads stdin (T-2080).
- `fm genesis`: the Genesis tower's home under the project's research/genesis. `fm genesis init --file F` saves gen-0 byte for byte and read-only, once; `fm genesis put NAME --file F` files a tower doc only in the tower's shape (gap-map.md, lineage.md, catalogue.md, report.md, exam.jsonl, gen-N/seed.md, gen-N/mission-NAME.md, gen-N/critique-NAME.md); `fm genesis` lists gen-0, the gap map, each generation's seed, missions and critiques, and the exam result count (T-2078).
- Brainstorms learn from what they missed: an ask the user had to make that no earlier idea predicted is listed in the next `fm ideas` pack, `fm mission` run and forever-loop round, with a "blind spots" lens that turns each into the question a brainstorm should have asked and asks it of the whole project; each lens ends with a `Next question:` for the next brainstorm, saved in ideas.json and carried into the next pack (T-2076).
- Forever-loop rounds list the files recent work left behind, learned from the repo's own history rather than a list of folders: a file that usually changes with the ones that just changed, but didn't, is listed with how many updates it owes; the brief also names every top-level area of the repo, and says so when git can't tell (T-2073).
- Audit briefs: a file with a changed line over 4000 characters (a recording, a minified bundle) is listed by its counts, not its text, so a re-recorded demo no longer fills the reviewer's diff (T-2069).
- Demo recorder: the live session runs with every plugin outside the foreman marketplace turned off (`--settings`), so another plugin's status line no longer shows in demo/live.mp4; Claude Code's startup notices (a disabled plugin's hooks.json warning, which `--settings` can't silence, and the login, plan and tmux notices) are blanked up to the Foreman panel, which stays intact (T-2055).
- Library lint: a card that runs `npx NAME` with a name not on the reviewed plugin/skills/library/npx-known.txt (the list beside the cards being linted) is a card problem; the electron, background-jobs and design-systems cards no longer name a missing package or an invalid stylelint rule (T-2049).
- Site: style.css and site.js URLs carry their content's hash, so browsers fetch the new ones; the hero's two lights drift slowly, the proof figures count up once, a feature card's light follows the pointer, and shader names wrap whole; all still under reduced motion (T-2063).
- Site: the landing runs four shader cards live (aurora bands, soft caustics, grain gradient, halftone) from each card's own GLSL, drawn only while on screen, one still frame under reduced motion and the card's words without WebGL2; it links to all eight (T-2062).
- Site: the fm code page draws its tour from today's TUI at every build (assets/code-tui-tour.svg, the still under reduced motion) in place of a video recorded once, lists every key from the app's own key map grouped as its keys poster groups them, and gains a block for the queue, asking beside a run, follow-ups, undo and PR, fm code why and --chaos; the README's demo links point at the site's tour (T-2061).
- Site: the tab icon's URL carries the logo's content hash, so a browser that kept the old favicon fetches the new one; the share image is a new card with the current logo (site/share.html, assets/share.png); the header logo loses its pixel-art rendering (T-2060).
- Looks: darkroom and glacier drop their hue-distance note; below accent chroma 0.03 a hue is no colour to clash with a status (T-2051).
- The pane replay SVG keeps a task title holding U+2028 on its own row: frames split on newlines only (T-2048).
- fm lib check lints the cards of the checkout it runs in (a builder's worktree), not the installed plugin's, so a builder no longer gets a false clean result; --root names another checkout (T-2047).
- A finished builder lane (its branch ahead, its tree clean, quiet for 30 minutes, its builder gone) is named for merge: fm next and the drive say fm lane merge ID instead of fm lane resume, so lane work no longer sits unmerged for days (T-2038).
- fm ship --watch and fm ci-watch wait for every workflow of the commit: a green pages run no longer reads as CI green while the tests run fails (T-2058).
- fm settings serve and fm inbox swipe --serve stop cleanly on SIGTERM: the signal sets a flag the loop reads instead of throwing into running code, which hung CI on Python 3.14 (T-2056).
- Site redesign: a two-column hero with a grid-and-glow backdrop and a highlighted headline, entrance motion, scroll-driven reveals, stamps that slam in, a scroll progress bar, the architecture diagram, hover lift on feature cards; every animation off under reduced motion. The hero's recorded video (whose terminal colours smeared) is now a session drawn as text in the site's palette, replayed line by line while its pane walks the eight stations, crisp at any size and in both themes, whole without JS or with reduced motion; the diamond poster and its WebGL scene became a plain ledger of the four totals. Every count on the landing page now comes from the build (tests, tasks, cards, areas, pages), no inline styles remain, and the demo reel (video, cast and SVG) no longer opens on Claude Code's folder-trust question (T-2040).
- README redesign: centered hero, a nav row, three hand-drawn diagrams (the loop, the guard, how the pieces fit) that read in light and dark, tables instead of paragraphs, depth folded into details; about 6,300 visible words (tables included) down to 1,700, prose 5,700 to 700. MASTER.md gains a contents line and two of the diagrams; a docs test keeps the README short (T-2052).
- New logo: a hard hat with a check mark on a navy tile, used as the site favicon, nav mark and README header (T-2041).
- Library: 25 UX pattern cards in a new ux area (onboarding, empty states, navigation, UX writing, error messages, search, filters, command palette, settings, pricing, checkout, sign-up, notifications, dashboards, progressive disclosure, wizards, undo, destructive actions, mobile navigation, tooltips, feedback, permissions, information architecture, usability testing, dark patterns) (T-2039).
- Library: 25 visual-design cards in the ux area (visual hierarchy, grids, spacing and type scales, palettes, tokens, icons, logos, brand, illustration, imagery, charts, landing pages, heroes, calls to action, social proof, bento layouts, gestalt, polish, handoff, art direction, app icons, share images, email) (T-2042).
- Library: 28 frontend cards for motion, effects and modern CSS (scroll-driven animations, view transitions, springs, anchor positioning, popover, oklch, and more) (T-2043).
- Library: 24 web cards for UI, motion and creative-coding libraries (GSAP, Lenis, Rive, lottie-web, React Spring, Anime.js, PixiJS, p5.js, Babylon.js, Spline, Theatre.js, Headless UI, Ark UI, Panda CSS, UnoCSS, Open Props, daisyUI, Mantine, Floating UI, Embla, Swiper, AutoAnimate, Tweakpane, Matter.js), each detected from package.json (T-2044).
- Eight more named looks on the style shelf, twenty in all (swiss-poster, darkroom, botanical-press, letterpress, cinema-lobby, glacier, saltpan, pit-stop), each with a measured palette and a gallery specimen; calibration pairs now show every look without repeating a rival (T-2045).
- Shader sketchbook (T-1784): fm scene sketchbook writes one page where every shader look card runs live with the preset its new Sketch section ships; thumbnails draw only while on screen, hold one still frame for readers who ask for less motion, and show the card's description without WebGL2. The site gains the same page.
- Change: the survival line (fm digest, fm outcomes) gives the number of closes and the share lost; '24 survived, 45 lost' read as a 65% loss rate when it was 45 of about 1300 (T-2037).
- Fix: a Python snippet drafted outside the project (in a scratch folder) is no longer blocked as unparsable; JSON drafts are still checked (T-2035).
- Fix: a test class run.py started now dies with run.py, even when run.py is killed outright (a Bash timeout); one orphaned class had spun a CPU core for a day (T-2034).
- Fix: the end-of-reply "tests pass" check no longer holds a reply that says when they will pass ("once the gates pass"), and an edit to a file outside the project, like a scratch draft, no longer makes the last green run stale (T-2032).
- Perf: the Stop hook's check for this session's running background work decodes only the event lines that hold its session id, not all 1,500 in the tail: Stop 88 -> 81 ms (T-2033).
- Perf: the active-task scan behind every tool call reads a small file of closed briefs' keys instead of the whole 700 KB brief index: PreToolUse 39 -> 36 ms (T-2031).
- Fix: quitting fm code while a runner test's CLI is starting ends that CLI too; before, a quit that landed before the app had seen the CLI left it running to its 60 s timeout (T-2030).
- Perf: the prompt hook's rewind check runs its three git commands only once the active task has a passing proof to take back, and uses the hook's one brief load instead of scanning the briefs again: UserPromptSubmit 94 -> 77 ms before a step is proved (T-2029).
- Perf: the daily risk budget check keeps today's counts in a small risk.json, so each hook reads only the ledger lines added since and runs git log only when HEAD moved. Its share of the Stop hook went from 9-10 ms to under 0.5 ms: Stop 60 -> 55 ms, UserPromptSubmit 98 -> 92 ms. A rolled or broken cache is counted afresh (T-2028).
- A done-when check that runs plugin/tests/run.py now warns when you write it if a named test module doesn't exist, or no test in it matches its -k filter. Such a check fails every run and can't be edited once it has evidence (T-2027).
- Perf: session start finds your last long silence in the ledger's recent lines instead of reading every ledger file, and filtered ledger tails search the raw bytes and parse only the lines they want. Session start takes 152 ms, down from 177, and the Stop hook 64 ms, down from 70 (T-2025).
- Perf: the prompt hook compiles patterns only for card names your message contains, compiles module patterns on first use, and loads briefs and the ledger's last edit once each. The median call takes 101 ms, down from 127 (T-2024).
- Perf: a hook loads `subprocess` and `glob` only when a call runs a process or searches for Python. The median PreToolUse call takes 41 ms, down from 44 (T-2023).
- Perf: the Notification hook takes 51 ms, down from 87, and PreToolUse 41 ms, down from 45. Closed briefs load from `briefs-index.json` in the project's state folder, checked against each file's inode, modification time and size, and a closed brief's file is read only when its body is needed. Loading 1,199 briefs takes 7.8 ms, down from 31.6 (T-2022).
- Perf: the PreToolUse hook takes 44 ms, down from 57, and the Notification hook 85 ms, down from 95. The hook modules' 191 module-level regular expressions now compile on first use, not at import; a PreToolUse call uses about 44 of them (T-2020).
- Fix: test runs no longer fill /tmp. Each test class gets its own temp folder, removed when it ends, and the next run sweeps any a killed run left. Leaked folders had used 99% of /tmp's inodes (17 of them held over 60,000 files each), so `git worktree add` failed with "No space left on device" and a ship was refused (T-2019).

- Perf: the PreToolUse hook takes 57 ms, down from 70, and the Notification hook 95 ms, down from 116. `plain()` passes printable text through without checking each character, a Bash command with no install word no longer loads the dependency check, and the last-change lookup parses only its own task's lines of the ledger tail: 5 ms, down from 21 (T-2017).
- Perf: `fm next` takes 143 ms, down from 187. It loads every brief once instead of up to five times, and each run builds only the parser of the command it runs instead of all 214 (T-2016).
- Perf: hooks load briefs and today's ledger events faster. A brief's sections are split only when read, and the frontmatter regexes are compiled once, so the first load of 1199 briefs takes 32 ms, down from 74. When the risk budget asks for today's events, it reads only the end of the 18 MB ledger: 2.8 ms, down from 30, with the same events (T-2015).
- Fix: a CI run that failed only because a job never got a runner (cancelled before any step: GitHub capacity) no longer files a FIX brief. `fm ship --watch` and `fm ci-watch` rerun the failed jobs and watch the new attempt; when gh can't rerun (logged out), they say to rerun on GitHub or push again (T-2013).
- Feature: `fm ship --task ID` ships on that task's publish grant, closed or active, so a finished task ships without a separate ship task. A prompt that ends a silence of 2 hours or more carries the away summary, not only a session start. A misspelled playbook name suggests the nearest ones ("did you mean fix/alpha?"). `fm check` and `fm ship` share one wait helper, which says when a wait runs out and it goes ahead beside the other run. The forever loop's stop decision, `fm loop off` and `fm loop` say how many rounds ran and tasks closed since it started (T-2012: T-2007, T-2008, T-2009, T-2010, T-2011).
- Fix: when `run.py`'s `-k` matches no test only because of case or underscores (`-k core_integrity` for `CoreIntegrity`), the error suggests up to three names it likely meant ("did you mean -k CoreIntegrity?"). With optional work dropped, step 2 of a forever-loop round names the lenses to cover across all of Foreman, with at least one grounded idea per lens, so rounds no longer shrink to follow-ups of the last ship (T-2005: T-2003, T-2004).
- Fix: `fm doctor`'s core-integrity check passes protected core the active task is changing inside its own scope, naming that task (every `fm check` of a core task warned "git checkout it back"); files outside its scope still warn. `run.py` names the `-k` that filtered a module's tests out ("no test matches test_run with -k X"); the docs-impact blocker suggests up to three tracked docs when several end with the named path (T-2001: T-1998, T-1999, T-2000).
- Fix: `run.py test_x -k NAME` runs only the tests of `test_x` that match `NAME` (names and `-k` were unioned, so it ran the whole module); the docs-impact blocker for a missing doc names the one tracked doc it most likely meant, as in `rules/foreman.md` → `(plugin/rules/foreman.md?)` (T-1996: T-1994, T-1995).
- Fix: an `@path`-only intake line under an item scopes that item instead of landing in untagged text; a task title keeps an `@path` written mid-sentence (only a leading or trailing one is a scope marker, as for `#T-` refs); the forever-loop brief says a small round with optional work dropped isn't counted dry (T-1992: T-1989, T-1990, T-1991).
- Fix: an edit during `fm ship`'s test run starts no ambient run beside it; a captured title is cut at a word with … and a command or identifier first word (`fm`, `c.first_touch`) keeps its case; a forever-loop round run while optional work is dropped no longer counts toward the dry streak that ends the loop (T-1987: T-1984, T-1985, T-1986).
- Fix: `fm ship` marks its scratch-tree test run and first waits for a running full `fm check` or ambient run here; a full `fm check` waits for a live ship test run (up to 10 minutes each, saying so), so two suites never run at once. `fm batch` keeps each member's DONE-WHEN verify command, `fm gates` names why-not-caught for an M or L FIX, and a Write outside the project (a scratchpad note) no longer counts as a CLEAN task's first edit (T-1980: T-1976, T-1977, T-1978, T-1979).
- Fix: one full `fm check` per tree: a second one waits (up to 10 minutes, saying so) for the live one and reuses its pass when nothing changed, and only the run that set the "gates running" marker clears it (T-1971). `fm risk` lists the guard rules that spend risk first, so routine habits no longer hide what spent the budget (T-1972). A full check whose tree changed while it ran starts one ambient run for those edits afterward (T-1973).
- Fix: the Stop drive, `fm next` and `fm ideas --deepen` say "optional work is dropped (<why>)" instead of "usage is ahead of pace (<why>)", so a spent risk budget is no longer called usage pace (T-1969).
- Never two test suites at once: a full `fm check` waits (up to 5 minutes, saying so) for a running ambient test run, and an edit made while full gates run starts no ambient run (T-1964, T-1965). A loop round run while optional work is dropped (usage ahead of pace, or the risk budget spent) skips the `fm ideas` fan-out for ideas made in the main thread (T-1966).
- Fix: `fm task finish` answers only for the closing task's own files: another task's uncommitted edits no longer run their linked tests at the close or trip the scope check (T-1968).
- Fix: a file changed while `fm check` ran no longer gets that run's pass or evidence; an edit made during a full check gets a note (T-1963).
- `fm task step ID edit N "<text>"` and `rm N` (T-1959); `fm ship --check [COMMIT…]` says whether main has those commits' changes without a grant (T-1960); fm focus's "Done needs" line names the task for `fm gates ID` (T-1961).
- Feature: `fm gates T-…` lists that task's gates; `fm task finish --numbers "<metric>: <before> → <after>"` records a PERFORMANCE task's measurements in the closing call; `fm task new --json --focus` keeps stdout to one JSON document (T-1954, T-1955, T-1956).
- Feature: `fm ship` lands finished commits on the remote's main in one call: it cherry-picks them onto the fetched main in a scratch worktree, runs the checks there and pushes only if main didn't move, behind the task's publish grant. A failure pushes nothing (T-1943). It remembers the last commit it shipped and starts after it, so commits main holds under another patch aren't offered again (T-1945); `--watch` then watches CI on the pushed commit (T-1946). It remembers the last `--test` too (T-1950).
- Feature: `fm ci-watch` works without `gh` (missing or logged out): for a public GitHub repo it reads the commit's runs and the failed jobs' annotations from GitHub's API, with no token, and briefs the failing lines as before. The ship-to-main playbook now names it (T-1939). When it gives up without seeing a finished run, it says it was reading the API (T-1940). A green run whose non-blocking job failed (continue-on-error, like the macOS jobs) no longer reads as plain green: ci-watch names the failed jobs and briefs them once, still exiting 0 (T-1942).
- Fix: a system-folder refusal for a path that holds "can't read" was mistaken for a program the guard never read, so a system grant couldn't lift it. Only the guard's own wording counts now (T-1934).
- Fix: in a script the guard reads, a refusal whose text happened to say "too deep" (a folder named so) was taken for the nesting limit and dropped. Only the nesting refusal itself counts now (T-1936).
- Security: the guard scans a script's `bash -c` or `eval` layers nested past its depth limit for what it always refuses (`rm -rf ~`, a pipe into a shell, a state write) instead of passing them unread. No grant lifts that refusal, and the message says to flatten the layers (T-1931).
- Security: a task's system grant no longer lifts the guard's refusal of a program it never read: text piped into a shell, words split on an IFS it can't follow, a script nested too deep, too long to parse in time or over the 256 KB it reads, or a script file passed to `sed -f` or `vim -S`. The grant would have run it unread. The message now says no grant lifts it and how to make the program readable (sed `-e`, vim `-c`), and the third identical refusal no longer says to ask for a grant (T-1932).
- Security: the guard judges a tree write after a cd it can't name in every folder bash may land in. Bash makes a name the line neither sets nor inherits empty: `cd "$X"` stays put, `cd $X` goes home (or to the next word), and `$X cd D` may run `cd D`. A computed command word (`C=cd; $C D`, `$(echo cd) D`, `C="cd D"; $C`) may be a cd. Nested text after a `cd` that `CDPATH` may resolve runs below each folder a `CDPATH=a:b` list names. An rm is judged in every folder the guard can name for the shell. Common lines such as `cd "$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)"` and `$PY -m pytest` still pass (T-1923).
- Performance: the guard judges a line of repeated cds it can't follow before `$(…)` bodies in under half a second instead of 7–17 s, which could time out the hook. A guessed folder is now tested once before it is joined with every place a lost cd may lead, with the same verdicts (T-1930).
- Security: a task's rm-outside grant no longer lifts the guard's refusals for a line too tangled to read to its end (nesting too deep, nested commands in too many folders, the line in too many folders, an `eval` of computed text). The rest of such a line was never read, so a grant let it run unchecked. Each refusal now names its way through: split the command, or run the text itself (T-1929).
- Fix: the code view's Activity, Run and Checks panels draw an event whose `text`, `cmd` or `action` isn't a string, instead of replacing the whole panel with a "panel … failed" line. The fuzz test now asserts that no built-in panel fails (T-1926).
- Clean: `fm task set ID interpretation=…` now says that Interpretation is a brief section and how to fill it (`--section 'Interpretation' --text "…"`). The `--section` help names the brief's sections, so `fm which interpretation` finds `fm task set` (T-1925).
- Performance: the guard judges a line of repeated `cd X; …; cd ..` blocks in milliseconds. Twenty such blocks never finished, and seven with `$(…)` in them were refused as "too many folders". A relative `cd` into a folder that exists is now certain, but only while every command before it on the line only reads. A `cd` run under a wrapper (`nice`, `sudo`, `env`) is never certain, because the wrapper runs it in a child and the shell stays put. This closes an older hole where `env cd /tmp && tar xf -` was judged from /tmp. The text after a cleanly closed `$(…)` is no longer re-read from every folder when every mover on the line is a plain `cd`. A line whose folder sets still pass 1024 entries is refused at once (T-1924).
- Fixed: the code view's time chart clamps a bar that starts before the run; such a bar padded gigabytes of spaces, and macOS killed the test run (T-1922).
- Security: the guard judges nested text (`sh -c`, `eval`, `sh <<<`, `find -exec`, `` `…` ``, `$( … )`) from the folders the line may be in when it runs, not the hook's folder: from a scratch folder, `cd <foreman> && sh -c 'git reset --hard'` and `cd <foreman> && python3 -c "os.system(…)"` got through. `cd <scratch> && echo $(ls) && git reset --hard` is no longer blocked as a tree write over Foreman, unless something after that head `cd` may move the shell (T-1921).
- The guard reads a `for`/`while`/`if`/`case`/`{ }` compound after `cd <scratch> &&` as one command run in that folder, so the cherry-pick loop its own hint suggests isn't blocked as a tree write over Foreman; a compound that may move the shell (a `cd` as written, quoted or escaped, a computed command word, a substitution) still makes the line branchy, and so does a command merely named like a keyword (`for-x`) (T-1920).
- A builder's contract says to probe code through the tests its criteria name, not `python3 -c 'import …'` from the root, where a module off the import path fails (T-1918).
- `fm task prove` leaves a changed tests-folder file that the start tree already has (a runner like `run.py`) at its start version, so the red run tests the change instead of passing with it; new helpers and test-named files still come over (T-1919).
- Guard lets a quoted heredoc write a scratch draft (`.txt`, `.patch`, `.diff`, `.md`, `.json`) that names Foreman's modules when the same command only feeds it to `fm edit` or an existing script, so writing and applying a draft is one command; any other use of the draft, a glob, quote, expansion or `cd` still blocks (T-1917).
- `fm which` and `fm help WORDS` name a command's actions too: "merge a lane" finds `fm lane merge`, not just fm lane's summary (T-1916).
- Guard treats `fm inbox ticket --post` (any spelling, a project flag first, or via `python3 …/fm`) as a publish, so an outward post needs the publish grant (T-1881).
- A guard block for a tree write over Foreman's own code (git checkout, reset or cherry-pick on its repo) says what passes: git in a scratch worktree, or fm edit per file with a standing core yes (T-1897). Interpreter code that drives Foreman's modules points a draft at the Write tool, then `fm edit <`. `fm risk` says how to start or focus a task for an edit made with none. Rule names drop filler words and read a contraction as one word: `core:tree-write-over`, not `core:a-tree-write-over`.
- Six new playbooks, written from finished work (T-1885). Routing 600 finished tasks against the library found kinds of work with no book or a wrong one; each now has its own, built from the tasks that did that work: command guard rules (`security/command-guard`), CLI errors and argument shape (`fix/cli-errors`), is the difference real (`perf/benchmark-statistics`), ship a branch to main (`release/ship-to-main`), regex backtracking (`perf/regex-backtracking`) and surface parity (`review/surface-parity`). Real titles of other tasks of each kind route their book in the top 3. Performance and security now keep a stage index, so the playbook list stays within its budget.
- fm capture's "already exists" warning sees subcommands too: a request restating `fm task prove` names it (T-1912).
- On CI, run.py ends a failed run with one error naming every failed class and its exit code, cuts long annotation lines, says "no output" for a class that wrote nothing, and annotates its own crash, so a failure is never just "exit code 1" (T-1911).
- `fm lib gaps` counts a Gradle `group:artifact` dependency as covered when a card names its bare artifact id, as the card spec says; the Espresso card also detects AndroidX Test and UiAutomator, and the Android card all of `androidx.lifecycle` (T-1914).
- A flaky test's note names the exception it raised (`AssertionError: …`), not the dashed rule that closes a unittest failure (T-1898). The site-hero scene probe runs in real time over DevTools, so virtual time can't run out its waits before a loaded machine draws (T-1898).
- The guard keeps a builder's write inside its worktree when the worktree is named through a linked parent folder (macOS's /var is /private/var), where it refused it as outside (T-1910).
- fm help with a command's name (fm help task, fm help task drop) prints that command's help, where "task" was refused as a word too common to look up (T-1909).
- fm which and fm help find task subcommands (fm task drop, fm task evidence) and the rules' plain words ("stop auto-continuing" finds fm drive), and fm which lists the strongest kind first; fm task -h says what each subcommand does (T-1908).
- fm task finish asks for the adversary lens on a security-sensitive change before any check runs, where fm task done asked for it only after the close's checks had run (T-1907).
- fm capture reads a leading tag as fm intake does: "FIX: the send button stays grey" is filed as a FIX titled without the tag, where it was a FEATURE with "FIX:" in its title, and `TAG!:` marks it urgent and `TAG?:` one to explore; --type still wins (T-1904).
- fm task step and fm task ac take a one-word step or criterion with no action named (`fm task step T-1 CHANGELOG`), where it was "invalid choice"; a word close to an action, as `dnoe`, still stops as a typo (T-1903).
- `fm task set --section` also reads a criterion written `done when :: cmd` with no checkbox and steps written one per line with no number, the forms `--ac` and `--step` take (T-1900).
- fm task finish refuses a FIX with no red→green proof before any check runs, where it ran the criterion checks first (over two minutes for a full suite) and then refused; a red run of a command the close is about to run still counts (T-1906).
- A builder's worktree and its main checkout count at their real paths too: on macOS, where the temp folder is a link, a builder's write inside its own worktree was refused as outside it, and the main checkout reached by its real path counted as scratch (T-1902).
- A test's cleanup retries for a few seconds while a detached child it started on purpose still writes into its folder; `test_session_start_runs_it_in_the_background` failed CI on Ubuntu with Python 3.14 with "Directory not empty" (T-1901).
- The drive's side-work offer skips a task that waits on any open task, not only on the active one, so a depends_on chain of drafted tasks isn't offered again on every Stop (T-1899).
- Many tasks in one command (T-1877). `fm task drop`, `defer` and the new `unblock` take a range (`T-3..T-7` or `T-3..7`: the tasks that exist in it) or a list (`T-3,T-9`), at most 50 at once. Each id is handled as if named alone; one that fails is said ("fm: task unblock T-0002: T-0002 is deferred, not blocked") and the rest still go, then "2 of 3 unblocked." with exit 1 when any failed. `fm task unblock ID [WHY]` moves blocked work back to planned, or captured while it has no criteria.
- Macros where they help (T-1876). `fm settings log` ends with a line for each key you set by hand three or more times — "border set by hand 3 times: fm settings macro border --set border=heavy saves it as one command" — unless a saved macro already holds that value; a panel move isn't counted, and the JSON is still just the log. `fm settings newcomer` names a saved macro that reaches a goal the newcomer got stuck on ("…; fm settings macro squared sets it", `macro` in the JSON).
- Guard collisions by rule (T-1875). `fm risk` lists the last 7 days' guard blocks by rule, most first, each with the rewrite that passes and "(spends risk)" when its category counts against the budget; a new session's first context names the rule that fired 3 or more times, with its rewrite. It only reports: the cap and its weights are unchanged.
- `fm task set --section` reads criteria written `done when :: cmd` and steps written `1. step`, the forms `fm task new` takes, and stores them as the brief keeps them (T-1894). Before, they stayed plain text, so the criterion had no verify command, the task had no steps, and `fm focus` refused it as not planned.
- `fm task finish` counts a red run recorded with `fm task evidence --expect-fail` as the red half of red-to-green proof (T-1893). Before, a fix built test-first that way was refused for having no proof, and `fm task prove` had to run the test again.
- The guard no longer reads `cd DIR && git checkout -- . && … || echo f` as a checkout over the folder it started in (T-1891). Any `||` in a line, including one in a loop body, made every `cd` in it uncertain. Now a command chained by `&&` after a `cd` is known to run in that folder. A `cd` after `||` (`true || cd X && y`) still counts as maybe skipped.
- `fm tidy` no longer asks to re-plan a queued task just because CHANGELOG.md is in its scope and changed (T-1890). Every finished task appends to the changelog, so four queued plans were flagged in one round for nothing. Other scope files still flag as before.
- The "fm check is piped here" note is given in full once per session (T-1889). Every compaction re-armed it, so the same two-line warning came back eight times in one session. After the first, a piped gate gets one line: read its ✓/✗ line, not the exit status. Session files named `<sid>.keep.*` now outlive a compaction, as `<sid>.taint.*` already did.
- `W=$(mktemp -d -p DIR)` is a known folder in DIR (T-1888). The guard read only a bare `mktemp -d` as a fresh temp folder, so `W=$(mktemp -d -p <scratch>) && cd $W && git cherry-pick …` was guessed as a write anywhere, the repo included. Now `-d -p DIR`, `-p DIR -d` and `-d DIR/nameXXX` are read as that literal path when DIR is literal and has no `~`.
- `S=/tmp/x && python3 - "$S/f.py" <<'EOF'` is read like a bare heredoc (T-1887). The guard took a quoted python heredoc as code it could parse only after lowercase `name=value;` prefixes, so this common edit-script shape fell to the text match and was refused as "driving Foreman's modules" whenever its strings named an fm module (5 times in one week). Now any variable name, separated by `;` or `&&`, is accepted, except the ones python or bash reads (PATH, HOME, IFS, LD_*, PYTHON*, CLAUDE*, FOREMAN* …), any already exported, since python would inherit its new value, and any command with `set -a`.
- A filled capture is planned (T-1886). A captured task given acceptance criteria and steps through `fm task set --section`, `fm task ac add` or `fm task step add` now becomes planned, as `fm task new` makes it. Left captured, the Stop hook kept offering it for planning after its brief was done. A status set by hand is kept.
- Tokens nobody uses (T-1874). `fm design lint` now ends with the DESIGN.md tokens no linted file uses — "DESIGN.md: 2 token(s) unused in the 14 file(s) linted: --accent-2, --shadow-deep" — so a palette that drifted from the code shows up. A definition isn't a use; a Tailwind theme name counts as used by its class (`--color-brand` by `bg-brand`). It only reports: the exit is the lint's. `--tokens FILE` names another tokens file; with no DESIGN.md or no `## Tokens` section it says nothing.
- A queue's quote before it starts (T-1878). `fm queue --cost` adds "Quote: 4 task(s) ≈ 1.2M tokens (each the median of its type and tier; no history: T-0012) · last 24 h 9.8M · week 41% used." Each task counts as the median of this project's finished tasks of its type and tier (two or more, last 90 days), so a long run is weighed before it starts. A quote only: nothing is refused.
- A failed gate says which test failed and how (T-1884): `fm check` names the failing tests (up to 5, then "+N more") and shows the first one's failure, not the last 10 lines of the summary.
- `--follow` no longer misses a change made right after a line is printed (T-1882). `fm ui`, `fm projects`, `fm sessions` and `fm claude` followers took their file-time snapshot after building and printing the view, so a capture that landed in between waited for the 30 s refresh (and made a test flaky). The snapshot now comes before each build.
- Every built-in scene at a glance (T-1873). `fm scene gallery --out sheet.png` draws metaballs, terrain, airlock, bench and lookbook small on one sheet, three across, and prints one caption per tile saying what it tells you. Each is built from this repo's data, or from a sample (with the reason) when there's none yet, such as no bench runs.
- Why an item sits where it does, and one door to the backlog (T-1872). `fm inbox prefer --why T-0042` says how far the choices `pick` taught lift or sink an inbox item (at most 2 points either way) and the title words behind it, one line each, largest first, adding up to its total: "onboarding +1 lifts it". `fm triage` opens the backlog surface that fits: the swipe loop in a terminal, the swipe page with `--serve`, otherwise the list of small items ready to start (`--json` too).
- Post a close back to its ticket (T-1870). `fm inbox ticket --post T-0042` posts one comment on the issue a done task came from (fetched by `fm inbox ticket`, or captured by `fm inbox gh`): "Closed in commit abc12345 (Foreman T-0042)." GitHub goes through your own `gh`, Jira and Linear through the same tokens as fetching. It runs only when you ask, once per task (two runs at once still post once; a failed post can be retried, and a timeout warns that it may have landed), only after the commit is pushed, and sends no ticket text. `fm task finish` of such a task names the command.
- GitHub issues in `fm inbox ticket` (T-1869). `fm inbox ticket gh:OWNER/REPO#7`, `'#7'` (this repo; quoted, since bash reads an unquoted `#` as a comment) or a github.com issue URL (a comment link too) fetches the issue through your own `gh` and plans it as one brief, as a Jira or Linear key does; an issue `fm inbox gh` already captured is that brief, kept as it is, and `fm inbox gh` skips an issue `fm inbox ticket` fetched. Fetching any ticket again after it changed now rewrites the brief it made while that brief is unstarted, instead of making a second one: a captured brief takes the new text, title, criteria and scope; a planned one takes the new text and title and keeps your plan. A started or closed brief keeps its own, and the change gets a new brief.
- Sturdier state (T-1879). A repeated `fm task defer`, `block` or `drop` with nothing new is a no-op ("T-0001 is already deferred.") instead of another log line, or a "reopen it first" refusal for a second drop. STATE.md and the other view files are rewritten only when something besides their Generated time changed, and STATE.md shows the tidy that just ran instead of "Last tidy: never". A new test keeps malformed briefs (cut-off frontmatter, binary bytes, wrong-typed fields, an empty file) from crashing the everyday read commands and hooks.
- Tickets by key (T-1859). `fm inbox ticket PROJ-12` fetches the issue from Jira or Linear and plans it as one brief, the same way a pasted ticket is planned. It also takes `jira:KEY`, `linear:KEY` or the issue's URL. Jira needs `JIRA_URL` and `JIRA_API_TOKEN`, plus `JIRA_EMAIL` on Jira Cloud; Linear needs `LINEAR_API_KEY`. The token is only ever sent to your Jira host or to Linear, and a Jira link on any other host is refused. Fetching the same issue again finds the brief it already made.
- Undo in the backlog sweep (T-1871). In `fm inbox swipe` in a terminal, u takes back your last answer and shows its card again; on the `--serve` page an undo button (access key u) and in the fm code app's backlog view (`I`) the u key do the same. A revived or dropped item goes back to deferred with its revisit date. An item changed elsewhere since, or a dropped batch, is left as it is and the undo says why. Each card now says how many are left, and an empty pile ends in one line. Both served pages share one stylesheet, which now follows the system's dark mode.
- The backlog in the browser (T-1858). `fm inbox swipe --serve` prints a link that opens the deferred pile one card at a time, with revive, keep, drop and skip buttons (access keys r, k, d, s). Each answer applies as you click it. The page runs no script and uses the same guards as `fm settings serve`: it listens on 127.0.0.1 only, its link works once in one browser, and it stops after 30 idle minutes. `/foreman:backlog serve` opens it from Claude Code.
- The backlog, anywhere (T-1866). `fm inbox swipe` in a terminal now asks about each deferred item in turn: r revives it, k keeps it, d drops it, s skips, q stops, and each answer applies at once. The fm code app has the same pile as its backlog view (key `I`). `fm inbox pick` no longer offers an item that waits on an open one, and now and then (`--epsilon`, default 0.1) pairs an item with a farther one of its kind, so your choices teach more than near-ties. `fm inbox prefer --reset` forgets what your choices taught; `/foreman:backlog reset` does it from Claude Code.
- Known fixes stop misfiring (T-1865). A fix learned from a failure is offered only for that same failure, not for any failure that happens to contain its text, and a signature too short to tell failures apart (`AssertionError: 0`) is never offered. Hand-taught fixes (`fm fix add`) still match inside a longer error.
- More from the taste ledger (T-1672). `fm looks roll` picks a look of the day, leaning towards the looks you kept, and the same one all day unless your taste changes; the pane shows it while idle. `fm looks bisect` says where your taste turned. `fm looks seance` lists the looks you turned away from, with what you said and the nearest look still in favour. `fm looks history` reads DESIGN.md's git history and records each look as kept (it stayed a week) or reverted (replaced sooner), once per stint.
- The settings Control Room grows three extras (T-1661). `fm settings serve` draws the app's layout as a grid of panels, each with ← ↑ ↓ → buttons that move it, the same move as the app's layout editor and one undo. Numbers turn by notches: a setting with a range shows as a dial with ticks, an open-ended one as a number with − and + buttons, and `[` / `]` turn the selected number in the app's settings screen. `fm settings newcomer` plays a first-time user through the settings screen using only what it shows, in a sandbox that writes nothing, and names the step and the missing hint where it got stuck (`--real` has a model play, `--goals FILE` your own goals).
- More `fm settings` (T-1658). `fm settings log [KEY]` lists every change and undo with what made it. An undo of a key you've undone three times or more says what keeps setting it. `fm settings macro calm --set theme=paper --set border=square` saves a named change; `fm settings macro calm` makes it as one undo step. `fm settings duel a.toml b.toml` draws two configs side by side on the same run. `fm settings replay` plays past runs, the sample run and a stress burst at four sizes; `--set border=double` shows what a change would break without writing it. `fm settings words "a calm light look" [--apply]` asks a model (through `claude`) for the settings that match, checks each one, and never proposes a change you've undone twice.
- Design tokens in and out (T-1652). `fm design tokens` reads a W3C design-tokens `.json` or a Figma variables export as well as DESIGN.md (top-level `light`/`dark` groups or a mode named dark are the themes; aliases are resolved). `--to md [--out DESIGN.md]` writes them as a `## Tokens` section, so Figma variables become DESIGN.md. `--to brand --out DIR` writes favicon.svg and a 1200x630 social card.svg in the accent, background and text colours. `fm design infer SRC` reads CSS and proposes a token for each colour, duration and radius written out twice or more, and lists literals a token already holds. `fm design xray PAGE` opens the page in headless Chromium, outlines each element green when every colour it paints is a token's and red when not, prints the coverage and the off-token colours, and exits 1 when any are off (`--out` keeps the shot). The design skills also ship as an optional `foreman-design` marketplace plugin.
- `fm task new --from` re-plans a planned task that was never focused, not only a capture: an M task whose `--focus` was refused for a missing Interpretation no longer needs `fm task set ID status=captured` to be planned again. A task that was started is still refused (T-1862).
- Three `fm critique` checks with no vision model (T-1644). `--bingo` scores nine tells of a templated look on a 3x3 card (a generic font, a violet accent, Tailwind's greys, one radius, one shadow, one gap, a flat or sprawling type scale, near-duplicate colours) and exits 1 on a full row, column or diagonal; it reads a page or a census `.json`. `--marks shot.png` takes a screenshot you drew on in pencil red, shoots the page again at the same size, and captures one FIX task for each marked element, named by its selector and text; `--note "…"` says what each mark asks, in reading order, and a rerun finds the same tasks. A JSON list of `{x, y, w, h, note}` boxes works too. `--brief [ID]` looks for the phrases a brief quotes in the page's text and exits 1 naming the ones missing.
- Backlog tools under `fm inbox`, and `/foreman:backlog` to drive them from Claude Code (T-1664). `fm inbox first` lists small items ready to start, with why. `fm inbox swipe` lists the deferred pile and `--apply ID:revive|drop|keep,…` settles it, all or nothing. `fm inbox pick` offers close pairs and `fm inbox prefer WINNER LOSER` records a choice: the inbox order learns from title words, at most 2 points either way, never past urgency or type. `fm inbox ticket FILE|-` turns a pasted ticket or error into one brief (type, title, criteria, a repro step, scope from trace paths), its text redacted, defanged and quoted. An L feature's first focus now says to spike first unless a step already does.
- `fm scene` refuses a flag the action doesn't read, naming the actions that do: `fm scene metaballs --days 5` used to run and drop `--days` without a word (T-1855).
- `fm scene shade card.md --out look.png` runs a fragment shader on the CPU and writes what it draws as a PNG (T-1846), so a shader card can be previewed with no browser or GPU. It takes a look card (its first fragment block) or a `.frag` file. `--size` sets the image (160x100 by default, up to 512 a side), `--time` sets `iTime`/`uTime`, and `--set uCol=1,0,0.5` sets a uniform. Unset uniforms take preview values that show the look, and the output names them. It runs a subset of GLSL ES 3.0: scalars, vectors, `mat2`–`mat4`, user functions with overloads, `for`/`while`/`if`, swizzles, `uint` maths that wraps at 32 bits, the common built-ins, and `dFdx`/`dFdy`/`fwidth` from 2x2 pixel quads as a GPU does. Shadertoy's `mainImage` works too. Anything outside the subset (textures, `#define`, indexing, `continue`, structs) is refused with the line it's on, counted from the top of the card. A pixel that divides by zero or discards is drawn black and counted.
- `fm scene lookbook` hangs the look cards in a 3D gallery (T-1852). Each look is a dark frame on the walls of a three-sided room, with its palette painted inside as colour-field bands from top to bottom. The best-ranked looks (`fm looks rank`) hang mid back wall, then on the side walls, then in a row above, all in the camera's view. `--count` sets how many hang (1-12, 8 by default). View it with `fm scene cells`, `turntable` or `compile`.
- `fm scene bench` draws `fm code bench`'s last 12 runs as a 3D diorama (T-1850). Each seed in each run is a box: runs go left to right and seeds front to back. A box is green when the seed closed and red when it didn't, and its height is log-scaled to the slowest run. Skipped seeds get no box, and runs from any model or variant share the window. View it with `fm scene cells`, `turntable` or `compile`.
- `fm scene airlock` draws the guard as a 3D gate (T-1849). Each category the guard blocked in the last 30 days (`--days`) is a warm box in front of the gate, named after the category. The tool calls it let through are one green box behind it. Heights are log-scaled, so a handful of blocks still shows next to thousands of calls. It works inside a Foreman project only. View it with `fm scene cells`, `turntable` or `compile`.
- `fm scene terrain` turns the repository's history into a 3D scene (T-1851). It reads the git log for the last 90 days (`--days`), takes the ten busiest top-level paths, and draws each one as a coloured ridge named after its path. Each ridge rises with the lines changed there over time, a column per day from the oldest commit in range to today (wider buckets past 40 days), scaled by log so one huge day doesn't flatten the rest. View it with `fm scene cells`, `turntable` or `compile`.
- `fm scene metaballs` makes a scene.json of a metaball blob (T-1848): 1 to 12 seeded balls (`--balls`, `--seed`) melted into one closed mesh that faces outward, on a grid of 8 to 48 cubes a side (`--grid`), over a floor and turning slowly. View it with `fm scene cells`, `turntable` or `compile`. Each cube is cut into six tetrahedra that agree on shared faces, so the surface has no holes, and no lookup tables are needed.
- `fm scene import three.json [--out scene.json]` turns a three.js scene saved with `Object3D.toJSON()` into a scene.json that `fm scene check` accepts (T-1847). It carries box, sphere, plane and buffer geometry, material colour, roughness, metalness and emissive, ambient, hemisphere, directional and point lights, the first perspective camera and the background colour. Each mesh's transform is composed through its parent groups. Anything scene.json can't hold (lines, textures, other geometry types, a mesh's second material) is left out and listed. With no `--out`, it prints the scene and lists what was left out on stderr.
- `fm page graph --cells` prints the task graph in the terminal: a box per task, coloured by status and as tall as its tier, drawn as 24-bit half blocks by `fm scene cells`, then a line naming each status's count. `--cols` sets the width; no page is written (T-1655).
- `fm scene cells scene.json` previews a scene in the terminal: fmraster draws it, and each cell shows two pixels as a 24-bit half block, so no browser or GPU is needed. `--cols` sets the width and `--turn` turns it about +Y (T-1635).
- `fm motion compile --to lab` writes a page that plays, scrubs and edits each motion in the Motion Score (T-1626). Drag a curve's handles or use the arrow keys, change a duration, and copy the score lines under it. `fm code` takes how long its bars, counts, columns and card ring settle from the score's `fm-code-*` motions, and keeps its own timing without them. The foreman-ui pane has no duration knob to take it, so it keeps its own feed and settle timers.
- `fm task finish` re-runs the failed command of a step whose last run was red, and closes the step if it now passes (T-1844). Before, it refused until a passing run was recorded by hand. A command stored with a secret redacted is still refused, not re-run.
- `fm task prove` names any changed non-test file in a tests folder (a runner, a helper) that it copied onto the start tree, if the check then passes there (T-1845). If that file is the code under change, the red run goes in the Regression test section instead.
- CI runs the suite in 2 shards per Python version (T-1842): `run.py --shard K/N` runs every Nth class, biggest first, so each job takes about half the time. One job had been running out of time at 92–100% of its deadline.
- A task active in a lane whose folder was deleted is named instead of "no active task" (T-1841): `fm status`, `fm next` and the prompt hook say which task, which lane, and that `fm lane rm ID` frees it.
- `fm drill` rehearses a bad day (T-1669): a scripted day of Foreman verbs and hooks in a temp home, with 3 to 6 faults planted mid-day (torn ledger or journal, stale lock, broken lane, a state file that isn't JSON, an unreadable file). It passes only when `fm fsck` or `fm verify-ledger` finds each fault or the day heals it, nothing crashed, and the day's task survives the repair; `--seed` replays a day. A corrupt or unreadable state file now ends a verb with one line naming it (exit 4), and hooks say so instead of failing with a traceback; journals no longer glue a new line onto a torn one. `fm fsck` names state files that aren't JSON.
- A design gate can be on `trial` (T-1669): it runs advisory, counts each new would-block, and starts blocking once 10 are logged with under 10% marked wrong (`fm design check --wrong GATE --why …`); enough wrong marks demote it again.
- `fm playbooks route` ranks real tasks better (T-1838): tuned on 24 real task titles, the use line weighs as much as the title, the query keeps a task's first 8 words, and `--task` prefers its type's folder (fix, perf, security, clean, ground) 1.5x; 17 of 24 land in the top 3, from 15.
- A `pgrep -f` or `pkill -f` whose pattern matches its own command line is refused before it runs, foreground or background: it found its own shell, so a wait never ended and a kill hit itself; the refusal names `kill -0 PID`, a bracketed letter, or run_in_background (T-1839)
- `fm playbooks route TEXT|--task ID [-k N]` ranks the playbooks by relevance over an incremental FTS5 index in the state folder (rebuilt if corrupt, pruned when its library is gone), with a title/use-line scan without FTS5; a mistyped one-word stage is a usage error; fm playbooks reach counts routes apart from reads, and a routed book is not a drop candidate; bench_playbooks.py times it at 20k (T-1833)
- Playbook use: a close records the books it loaded and the share followed; `fm playbooks coverage --unused` names drop and merge candidates; a draft repeating a book is refused (`--force`); the skill list is capped (T-1834)
- The site has a Gallery (T-1785): one worked page per design playbook (motion, interaction, game feel, sound, type and colour, composition, design direction, data viz, scrollytelling, terminal UI, visual QA, and the 3D template), each a small real screen that applies its playbook and passes `fm new check`. Each playbook names its page on an `Example:` line, under `references/ui/examples/`, so you can open it from disk too.
- `fm check --python oldest` (or `--python 3.12`) runs the gates under the oldest Python Foreman supports, as `python3` (T-1805): run it once before a push to main or a release to catch a break only that version has, which CI found days late before. Every other `fm check` stays as fast as before.
- On macOS, quitting the `fm code` app now names and stops each runner its fm code started (T-1828): it read runner names from /proc, which macOS lacks. Playbook code blocks there run in the resolved temp folder, and the macOS CI job no longer hangs on terminal tests that read output after the program closed it.
- `fm run` no longer pays for a session that would repeat the last one (T-1825): when a session leaves the files and the failing check exactly as it found them, the task is blocked with that check named and the run moves on. A session's first recorded check now counts as progress (it was missed, so such a session read as no progress).
- A builder's work can be dropped and redone from what went wrong (T-1823): `fm lane rm ID --why "…"` keeps its branch and the reason, and the next builder brief starts with the reason, the branch and the check that was red.
- `fm cost` shows each task's retry tax (T-1824): the tokens spent between a step's failed check and its next pass. `fm cost --task ID` puts it beside spent with its share; the by-task line names it too. A test-first step's deliberate red run doesn't count.
- Every `fm code` app command now has a route in Claude Code, checked by a test (T-1822): a `/foreman:` skill or command, an `fm` verb (each subcommand checked), git, or a Claude Code key, or it's named app-only with a reason (moving around panels, the photo booth, Breakout). A new app command without one fails CI.
- `fm pause --now` stops what runs, not only what would start (T-1821): every fm session and the tools it started, every fm serve unit, every file lease, and a running `fm run` or `fm night` stops its session within 2 seconds (lanes with work are kept). Each `fm pause --now` writes one record to `state/stops.jsonl`, also when it runs inside a session or serve unit it stops. Interactive Claude Code sessions and subagents still finish their turn. `fm pause off` lifts it as before.
- `fm cost`, `fm digest`, `fm budget` and `fm serve` price 1-hour cache writes at 2× base input, Anthropic's price, instead of the 5-minute cache's 1.25× (T-1820). Sessions on the 1-hour cache were shown about 10% cheaper than they were. `fm cost --sessions` labels a cache bust "ttl" only when the gap outlasts that row's own cache lifetime (an hour when its last cache write was a 1-hour one). It also shows how many input-equivalent tokens each session's busts cost and what causes each kind.
- `fm docs` and `fm tidy` no longer report example paths in stack cards (a card with a `detect:` line describes the projects it detects, such as `./gradlew`) or paths in changelogs (history names files as they were) as broken doc links; MASTER names the bench cases file's state folder (T-1817).
- Changes to CI workflows, hook configs (`hooks/hooks.json`), Claude Code settings, install scripts and pre-commit or husky hooks now need the adversary lens like auth code does (T-1818): they run with CI's secrets, on install or on every tool call, outside the diff's own tests.
- CI names what makes a job slow (T-1809): `run.py --deadline S` ends the run after S seconds, killing classes still running (with their stacks) and failing unstarted ones, so a job ends itself before its cap instead of being cancelled silently; on GitHub Actions the run's time and five slowest classes go out as a public notice, with a warning past 75% of the deadline. The workflow sets it 2 min under each job's cap, and Ubuntu's cap goes from 20 to 25 min (its suite takes 790–934 s, too near an 18 min deadline). A deadline kill says "at the run's deadline", apart from the 480 s per-class cap; unstarted classes are named on stderr, CI or not; `--deadline 0` is refused.
- Claude Code 2.1.305–312 (T-1797): every Sonnet agent (reviewer, recon, reproducer, debugger, builder) compacts at 150k, which from 2.1.312 lowers Foreman's 200k window for subagents, and fm doctor checks the value and the version; `fm cost --sessions` flags a session averaging over 200k context a reply (the window isn't applied); the usage-burn warning points at `/usage`, whose `f` applies Claude Code's own fixes.
- Session start names problems nobody raised (T-1810): CI red on main (one FIX per red commit), a changed model, window or Claude Code version (the cost math reruns), a stale blocked pile and an overdue tidy, each with its action, from caches a detached sweep fills every 10 min; every review multiplies cost at scale.
- Less usage burn (T-1796): fm install-user now sets CLAUDE_CODE_AUTO_COMPACT_WINDOW=200k, so with the 70% override a session compacts near 140k tokens instead of near 700k on a 1M-context model (every reply re-reads the whole context: Foreman sessions averaged 384k a reply). Every builder lane runs on Sonnet, M tasks included, and compacts earlier (autoCompactWindow 150000); the main thread still reviews and merges. Restart running sessions to pick up the window.
- Pane frames (T-1670): mods/foreman-ui/tools/pane_replay.py svg draws a replayed Foreman pane as an SVG or PNG, and the pane now has a committed golden frame: the mod's own tests fail when the pane draws differently, until pane_replay.py golden --update records the new look.
- Desk card and moving charts (T-1615): fm desk draws the project's status as a black-and-white card for an e-paper panel or an old phone (SVG, PNG with rsvg-convert, or an HTML page that reloads itself), once or every few seconds into a synced folder; no port is ever opened. The site's bench and stats charts now draw themselves once as they scroll into view, and stay still for readers who ask for less motion.
- Settings scopes (T-1611): in the fm code app's settings screen, s picks where a change goes: everywhere (tui.toml), this project, or this run only; :set takes --scope too, and fm settings set --scope project works from a shell. A repo can pin look settings in .foreman/policy.toml ([pin]) over every scope; keys and motion are never pinned, and a pinned setting says so instead of changing.
- Playful extras (T-1556): fm extras zine folds an fm code run into a printable one-sheet, eight-page zine; fm extras vase turns a run into a closed vase STL for a slicer's vase mode; fm extras cursor X,Y … draws an animated cursor for demos; B in fm code opens Breakout while a run's checks run (it closes when they stop and gives every other key back); the directions skill gains a sonic UI recipe set, off by default.
- Marks (T-1555): fm marks icons writes 19 icons on one 24 grid with one stroke (round caps, currentColor) and an icons.svg sprite; fm marks empty draws four empty states from the same icons; fm marks wordmark NAME sets the name beside a monogram tile in a hue drawn from the name; fm marks fonts lists vetted open-licence pairings marked against what is installed, and --subset makes a self-hosting subset when fontTools is there (never installed for you).
- Scaffolding and dev loop (T-1554): fm new web DIR (or scene) into a new folder makes a project born proven: git init, fm init, the template's own check as its gate, a first commit, a passing fm check; fm dev runs fm check --affected whenever a tracked file changes; fm codemod rename OLD NEW and fm codemod sub PATTERN REPL rewrite tracked text files, showing the diff first and writing only with --apply
- Session and release care (T-1549): the brainstorm pack holds each of the user's quotes once; fm release warns when it starts in an hour whose gates this project's record shows failing at least twice as often as usual; each release's notes name every default it changes (plugin/defaults.json keeps the last release's), and fm settings changed says which of those reach you because you never set them; fm digest --say reads the digest aloud with an installed speech program
- Receipts and review (T-1547): fm task receipt ID --svg FILE draws the receipt as a blueprint sheet (files in reading order with their added and removed lines, the swatches it added, its before/after); --sign KEY signs it with your SSH key and prints the ssh-keygen -Y verify line that checks it; and fm audit prep puts the nits earlier reviews found in the same files into the review brief as a pre-review checklist
